# The girls

Amy and Jess: how they decide, notice and react, what they do, you and them, their bodies and clothes.

## Where things stand

What is true now, with the sections below to read for the detail. Keep it current: an item that changes how something stands fixes the line here as well as adding its section.

- **How their minds work** (the method is in `.claude/rules/girls-minds.md`). Seven needs (warmth, company, excitement, rest, something to do, and your attention) rise and fall with what's around her. Every couple of seconds while she's free, the chooser scores each `OFFER_KINDS` offer by the needs it meets times her weight times a whim, and she takes the best above 0.5. Shelter is urgent and can interrupt something idle. Everything she decides with her free time is an offer. Things that answer an event (your rev, being left on a quad, falling out) stay reactions on their own clocks. "Needs and a chooser (TODO 133, with 134): what was built", "Needs and a chooser (TODO 133): the rest moved"; the plan sections before them are history.
- **Around the chooser:** a mood that lasts minutes, eased from her unmet needs, nudging pace and keenness without ever refusing; memories of outcomes that scale an offer kind's weight (carried at half strength); now and then a second thing strung on as a plan; a daily agenda dealt from her odds; and `pace` per offer (hurry, brisk, dawdle) with interrupt and resume. "Moods that last (#14, was TODO 119)", "Memories that change choices (#15, was TODO 120)", "Plans, not just picks (#16, was TODO 121)", "Her own day (#20, was TODO 125)", "Going about it (TODO 135)".
- **Is she free?** One answer, `availability()`, with `free`, `canRemark`, `asked` (soft jobs give way when you ask) and `mine`. A feature holds her with `hold`/`release`, and `worldHold` holds both girls. Asking her is a priority, and the wheel's general requests break into whatever she's doing. "Whether a girl is free (TODO 132)", "Asking the girls is a priority (2026-10-11)", and the wheel in interface.md.
- **Attention and reactions.** What she looks at is scored in one place from her state's `look`, notices (`game.notice`, `NOTICE_KINDS`), what's around her and her interest. A rev needs the throttle open and a fifth of the engine's range. Reactions (flinch, step back, cover ears, gasp, cheer, turn away, wave, pose) come from `NOTICE_REACTIONS` or `react()` and are posed last over everything. Sound decides what she notices behind her. "The girls' attention (TODO 130)", "Revs, not idling (TODO 136)", "How the girls react (TODO 131)", "Sound shapes what they notice (#22, was TODO 127)".
- **The two of them.** One with more need for company goes and stands with the other, they face each other, and the circle opens when you walk up. One may tag along on a wander, a sit down or tea. They're rivals for you, so their banter is snide (Amy sweet on top with an edge, Jess openly competitive). They fall out and make up. Neither talks over the other. "The two as a pair: as built (TODO 138)", "Falling out (TODO 4)", "Making up (#21, was TODO 126)", "The girls talk over each other and start in step (issue #2, was TODO 107)", and the banter in process.md ("Next: getting stuck, weather, and the girls talking").
- **You and her.** Favour is a score per girl. What matters is her standing against her friend: favoured or neglected past 0.35, which changes lines, ride offers and (only when neglected) the odd refusal. Jealousy is a need. Romance comes from a high favour (two visits round the back of the barn, then her quad), a lock-in that holds her favour, and betrayal by tipping her off. Half of favour carries overnight. "Who you favour (2026-10-05)", "Jealousy as a need (#18, was TODO 123)", "Round the back of the barn (TODO 6)".
- **What they do.** Ride on the back, ride on the front rack, sit side-saddle, drive (with a confidence that sets pace and nerve), follow you, race, drag, run convoys, do doughnuts, jumps and the climb, show off, prank, nick your quad, bring tea, warm their hands, shelter in the barn or a cab, sleep on the back, take you to see something, call for help when bogged or tipped, give and take driving lessons, practise the clutch alone, work at the bench, carry things, take their own cars out, load hay, sit by the fire, and fetch jumpers and coats. Each has its own section, named after it.
- **Who they are** (`characters.js`): Amy is careful, knows engines (`diagnoseAccuracy` 0.9), tidy and cold-blooded; Jess is restless, cheeky, a worse judge (0.5) and loud. Differences are weights and rates, never rules. Each owns a quad she cares for, and each girl names the quads. They only ever call the player "you". "Personalities, a companion wheel, and bolder girls (2026-10-05)", "Nicknames, whose quad, and hearing one (TODO 9, 10, 11)", "Getting attached to a quad (#23, TODO 128)".
- **Bodies and clothes.** One CC0 model, each girl with her own figure and height. Garments are shells made from her own skinned triangles, cut along smooth lines where an edge must fall (the polo). The day's outfit is dealt from the farm seed and the day (polo, plaid, double polo, popped collar, a shirt and tie, Jess's crop-top on a hot day), with jumpers, wax jackets and helmets on top. Girls stay full size on scaled vehicles. "Two different figures, a rounder bust, and the seat of the shorts (2026-10-14)", "The polo as a garment (spike, behind `?polo=shell`)", "Other outfits, and layers (TODO 204)", "A shirt and tie (#489)", "Wax jackets and hoods (TODO 62, first half)", "Helmets and goggles (TODO 48)". The collar's earlier goes ("The polo collar, third go" to "No collar; rounder still") are history.
- **The start of the day** is a briefing: Amy reads out the weather, the board, the quad playing up and each girl's plan. "A start-of-day briefing (#45, was TODO 150)".
- **Their voices** are in sound.md.

---

The sections below are the record, oldest first. A new decision goes at the end.

## Interaction and NPC

- Interaction targets are invisible proxy meshes. A direct ray hit wins; otherwise anything whose bounds come within 8 degrees of the view direction counts. Without that, standing upright next to the quad, your eye line passes over it and you'd have to look down to get a prompt.
- The companion's walking is direct-path with ray probes at knee height for steering: if the way ahead is blocked she tries angles either side, sticking to one side for a moment so she doesn't dither. If she makes no progress for 5 seconds she ignores obstacles and pushes through. Checked routing round the barn.
- She's parented to the quad's pillion anchor while seated, which sits inside the vibrating body group, so she shakes with the bike for free. Her spine also gets a small sway from the vibration output.
- Hedges don't cast shadows. Hundreds of instances made the shadow pass the biggest single cost.

## Smoke and outfit (2026-10-04)

- Exhaust smoke is a pooled point-sprite system with one puff per firing pulse, capped at 45 puffs a second; at high revs pulses merge into fewer, denser puffs. Puffs are kept above the terrain because point sprites depth-test at their centre, and big puffs half in the ground looked like stains. Proper soft particles would need a depth pre-pass; not worth it yet.
- The companion's outfit is data in `src/npc/outfit.js` (`OUTFITS`). The model's texture is a six-colour palette, so clothes are recoloured by swapping palette entries. The polo collar points and placket are placed by raycasting her skinned mesh to find the chest surface, then attached to the upper spine bone. The navy is lighter than true navy (#3a5690) because darker values read as black under the overcast lighting.

## Companion polish and roadside controls (2026-10-04)

- She's 1.5 m tall now (was 1.66 m).
- Collar and wellies are fitted to her body instead of sized by hand: `fittedShell` in `src/npc/outfit.js` casts rays in towards a bone's axis from all round, finds the surface, and builds a shell a few millimetres outside it. The collar uses the hit nearest the axis (so hair is ignored); the boots use the outermost hit with a short reach (so the jeans hem tucks inside, and the other leg isn't caught). Shells are built in the rest pose and parented to the bones.
- She glances at the player on a random timer (4 to 11 s apart, 1.5 to 3.5 s each), looks at you while you're talking to her, and doesn't bother while you're riding with her behind you. Head turns are split 40/60 between neck and head, layered after the animation.
- Seated on the quad she fidgets every 3 to 8 s: lifting an arm, leaning, stretching or looking out to the side. Standing, she occasionally leans or looks around, half as often. All are procedural bone offsets with ease in and out, so they work on top of any clip.
- While looking at the quad on foot, K, R, Q and Z work its ignition, starter and choke, and the engine status shows. The quad only listens while it's the focused interactable.
- The player no longer shoves the quad by walking into it. The character controller's impulses on dynamic bodies are off.
- Misfires no longer drop the sound out. A misfire's pulse now plays with its opening thump held down (25%) while the rest of the cycle plays at 70%, because a real misfire loses the bang but the engine keeps turning. Every pulse also runs 5% longer than its predicted cycle, so a late next pulse crossfades over it instead of leaving a gap. Warm idle now measures the same as the original recording for short dropouts (none).

## Her figure (2026-10-05)

- Request: a slightly more hourglass shape with a big bust. Her body mesh (the Quaternius model) is reshaped once at load in its bind pose (`src/npc/figure.js`): a fuller, rounder bust, a narrower waist and wider hips, as smooth regions placed relative to her own skeleton, so they scale with her height and every animation (idle, walking, sitting, mounting) carries the shape without changes. It runs before the outfit is fitted, so the collar, placket and wellies follow the new body; the polo itself is part of the body's texture.
- Tunables `bust`, `waist` and `hips` in the Companion folder (0 is the original model, default 1, up to 2) reshape her and refit the clothes live. The model is low-poly with flat shading, so the shape is as round as its triangles allow.

## Her starting quads, her own mind, more smoke, parked creep (2026-10-05)

- Request: ask her to start a quad. `StartingAttempt` (`src/npc/startingAttempt.js`, pure, tested against the real engine and fuel models) is a novice's routine: key on, the tap if she remembers (75%), choke if it's cold (80%, and 15% when it's warm, which floods it), cranking in 2 to 4 s goes with pauses, remembering what she forgot between goes (55%), and giving up after four goes with the ignition off. Once it catches she eases the choke off as it warms, then hands it over. She holds the starter through `quad.helperStarter`. In tests she gets a cold quad going with the tap off most of the time but not always, and has it warm with the choke off in about two minutes. She talks herself through it, and her usual "have you tried the choke?" hints are muted while she's the one cranking.
- Request: when left idle for a while she should do things on her own, but stay on a quad once she's on one. After 55 to 90 s standing about (not seated, not being talked to) she picks: get on a quad within 40 m (35%), have a go at starting one (30%), or wander 6 to 18 m for a look round, saying what she's up to. Seated, she never gets bored off. `ownMind` turns it off.
- Feedback: still not enough smoke. Master opacity 1.4 (from 1.0), every density up by roughly a third to two thirds, puffs live up to 5 s and grow to 2.3 m, and the blue's and yellow's overrides in step.
- Found while testing: parked quads crept down the yard's 6 to 8% slopes at about 1.2 cm/s (a metre a minute), engine off, since the wider spawns. The raycast wheels' brakes don't fully hold, and neither more brake, more side grip nor body damping changed it. Now a parked quad (nobody driving, no throttle, nearly still) has its horizontal velocity cancelled each step once it's below `parkedHoldSpeed` (0.15 m/s); it still settles and bounces vertically. Measured: no drift at all over 35 s.
- Her lines are re-recorded with the cache: 376 lines, 57 new. The voice bank is 24 MB at 22 kHz.

## Pillion body language, and a braking bug (2026-10-05)

- Riding pillion, her body language is a posing layer (`src/npc/pillionPose.js`) on top of the sitting clip, so the clips and the seated state are unchanged. Her hands are placed with two-bone IK (`armIK.js`) onto grip points: round your waist when you're in front (within 1 to 3 cm of the target), the rails on the utilities, the yellow's strap only when it's moving (her thighs when it's parked). Leaning, tucking, the braking lurch and bumps come from damped springs driven by the quad's own acceleration (`pillionDynamics.js`, tested): she leans up to 0.3 rad in a hard turn and about 0.35 rad forward under hard braking, lagging the bike slightly and settling rather than snapping.
- Look back: hold C or the middle mouse button; the view turns 205 degrees over your shoulder in 0.35 s with the eye moved out over the shoulder, and she sits up and looks at you.
- Bug found while testing: after the engine-load merge, hard braking only managed about 1 m/s2 (it was about 10). Rapier's raycast vehicle ignores a wheel's brake whenever that wheel has any engine force, and the new overrun engine braking left a small negative drive force on. Drive force is now zeroed whenever the brake is above rolling resistance: braking is back to 9.5 to 10 m/s2.
- Known: there's no rider body, so from outside her hug clasps an empty seat; from the rider's view it works.

## Her driving (2026-10-05)

- Request: she drives a quad herself, when bored or when asked; not far, not fast; bored or scared after about a minute; then off or onto the back; on the back she only gets off when asked. Built by an agent that was interrupted near the end and finished by a second one from its branch.
- She drives through the same control path as the player: `Quad.setDriver()` gives the quad an object whose throttle, brake, steer, starter and reverse are read each physics step in place of keys, so engine, drivetrain, mud, smoke, sound and vibration behave as they do for you. The brain (`HerDrive` in `src/npc/herDriving.js`, pure, tested) is a slow, wobbly speed controller steering round a small randomised loop (4 to 6 waypoints, 5 to 9 m radius) through her start point, kept clear of things with raycast probes and inside the hedges. Novice touches: wobbly throttle, yanking the bars, braking to look round, forgetting the choke (once: a stall teaches her), rolling back on a slope (she now notices, brakes and gives it more), and giving up if it keeps stalling. After 45 to 85 s she pulls up (bored, or scared 40% of the time) and gets off or moves to the pillion.
- Measured over 16 headless runs (all quads, cold and warm, grass, track, by the hedge, by the barn): no collisions, top speeds 3.2 to 4 m/s (average 1.6 to 2.1), at most 18 m from where she started, about a minute on the front. Stops take about a second from 2.5 m/s rather than stamping on the brake.
- Her front-seat pose is its own rig poser (hands on the grips through the shared arm IK), kept apart from the pillion pose layer. `boredDriveChance` 0.25 joined the boredom choices (getting on the back 0.3, starting one 0.2, otherwise wandering). You take the quad back by getting on, from the menu, or with E on her quad while it's moving; the menu reaches her from 14 m while she drives.

## Personalities, a companion wheel, and bolder girls (2026-10-05)

Decided with you before building:

1. Each quad gets a fixed character with only small per-game variation: red 1,050 to 1,150 rpm idle, roughness 0.04 to 0.12, gentle shake (the smooth one); blue 1,400 to 1,550, 0.2 to 0.3, medium quicker buzz; yellow 1,500 to 1,800, 0.35 to 0.5, fine high-frequency buzz through the bars; green 800 to 900, 0.55 to 0.7, big slow heave like a tractor; orange 2,000 to 2,300, 0.75 to 0.9, harsh and hard. Wear still builds on top; a service brings a quad back to its own character.
2. The girls' menu becomes a two-ring companion wheel: quads (and anything not about a quad) first, then that quad's options. The menu on the back of a quad becomes a one-ring wheel too.
3. Boredom much more random (wide spread of waits, drifting restlessness, choices reshuffled each time) and confidence strongly raises how often she drives. A fully confident girl drives at around 25 to 30 mph (you chose this over the 20 mph I suggested), for longer, in big loops across the field.
- 1, built. The ranges are as agreed. Shake was tuned per quad and measured from the vibration model at each quad's mid idle and roughness (RMS body heave / handlebar movement): red 2.0 / 0.7 mm, blue 3.6 / 1.5 mm at about 12 Hz, yellow 3.3 / 2.5 mm (the most bars for its body), green 12.5 / 4.8 mm at about 7 Hz, orange 10.2 / 4.9 mm with more random kick directions. A first go had the green at 26 mm: the roughness term grows with its cube, so a rough quad's gains need to be lower.
- The toolbox idle screw used the bottom of `idleRange` as its safe stop, which narrow ranges would have squeezed to almost nothing; specs now carry an explicit `safeIdle` (the old safe minimums) for it.
- New test (`quadCharacter.test.js`): every quad at both ends of both ranges, new, half worn and fully worn, idles warm for two minutes with blips without stalling, and new or half worn survives a hard stop every 10 s (a full second of braking load). Six seeds each, checked once at twelve. The green stalled on hard stops at half wear until its braking drag (`loadDragRpm`) came down from 2600 to 1700, which fits its heavy flywheel. Fully worn quads, the red included, can occasionally stall on a hard stop, as before; that's left in as a reason to service them.
- The old ranges overlapped so much that any quad could roll as glassy or as rough as any other; that's what blurred them.
- 2, built. The wheel (`src/interaction/wheel.js`) replaces the list menu everywhere it was used (the old `menu.js` is gone). Mouse movement while it's open drives a virtual cursor (it reads the mouse before the player's look does, so the view doesn't move); past a small dead zone, the slice in that direction is picked. Numbers, the scroll wheel and arrow keys work too; click, Enter or Space chooses; right-click, Backspace or Escape goes back a ring; E or Q closes. Her actions now carry the quad they're about and a short label, and the game groups them: one slice per quad (nearest her first) with a band of its colour, and actions not tied to a quad on the first ring. If there's only one quad slice and nothing else, it opens straight into that quad's ring. Opening it puts the help panel away, because the slices are see-through and the help showed through them. Tested headless: mouse to the right and down picked the red quad, clicking opened its ring, right-click went back, and 1 then the number for "Drive it" sent her off to drive.
- 3, built. Boredom (`src/npc/boredom.js`, tested): each wait is drawn on a log scale around her usual wait (60 s for Amy, 45 for Jess), so two in three fall between about 35 s and 100 s and the rest anywhere from 12 s to 200 s; it's divided by a restlessness that drifts between 0.5 and 2 on its own. Each time, the odds of what she does are multiplied by a random whim, and driving by an appetite that grows with the square of her driving confidence (0.3 careful, 0.9 middling, 2.7 sure of herself): in tests she picks driving under 12% of the time careful and over 45% at full confidence. A quarter of her strolls are two and a half times as long. A good drive builds her confidence by 0.1 (was 0.08).
- Confidence (`driveMood.js`): every param is unchanged at 0.5 and moves steadily towards careful below it; above it the bold end comes in slowly then quickly (to the power 1.6), so real speed is for when she's really sure: top cruise about 13 mph at 0.7, 20 at 0.85 and 29 at 1, with a scare speed of 36 mph, full throttle, loops of 17 to 31 m radius, 2.5 times as long out, hardly any pauses or overcorrecting, and much less often scared.
- Her driving brain was built for walking pace, and at speed it would have tipped the quad or hit things, so it now: caps the bars to what keeps cornering at about 3.5 m/s2 (more when confident; at 13 m/s full lock would have been about 4.5 g); slows for a turn to what that allows given how far it has to turn and how far away the waypoint is; looks 2.2 s ahead instead of 1; slows for things ahead no harder than 3.5 m/s2 (the low-speed creeping law is unchanged); accepts a waypoint within half a second's travel; and looks to either side, capping her speed at 1.2 m/s per metre of room when she's among things. That last one came from the first full-confidence runs, where both girls bumped parked quads at 4 to 7 m/s pulling out of the yard; with it, a run of three pairs of drives had no bumps, top speeds of 20 to 28 mph and up to 72 m out. You chose not to verify further and to report anything you see in play.

## Following when you stop, and the help panel (2026-10-05)

- You asked whether a girl following you stays on her quad when you stop and get off. She did, indefinitely: following counted as carrying someone, so she never tired, and with you off your quad she just held it on the brake. Now, with you off your quad (or on hers) for `followGiveUpMin..Max` seconds (30 to 90; Jess 15 to 50), she stops following and either drives about on her own from where she is for a go of her usual length (`followDriveOnChance` 0.35, scaled with her confidence like her other driving params, 0.5 for Jess) or pulls up and finishes as after any drive. Each has its own shouted lines. While you're still on your quad, however long you've stopped, she keeps waiting.
- The help panel no longer opens at the start of every game; H shows it, as the start screen says.

## Both girls on one quad (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: both ways of it happening (you ask, or a bored girl hops on as her friend sets off, never onto a moving quad by her own choice); the driver gives a proper ride (bigger loops, slower to tire) but stops after a few minutes and both get off; each reacts with her own usual lines, no conversation between them yet.

- Claims are per seat now: `claimedSeat` is 'rider' (walking to start or drive it, starting it, getting on the front, driving), 'pillion' (walking to get on, getting on the back, seated) or 'both' (sliding between seats). The other girl leaves a claimed seat alone but can use the free one. `canRidePillion` also keeps her off a quad whose driver is following you or waiting to give you a ride.
- The driver's brain gets a `friend` hook ('coming', 'on' or null): coming, she pulls up (parks) or waits before setting off; on, she says so, replans a loop 2.5 times bigger and keeps going for `friendRideMin..Max` (90 to 200 s) from then. The passenger gets off 1 to 3 s after her friend has got off at the end. Getting off together, the second one lands on the other side.
- The girl on the back now gets the pillion ride remarks (fun, rough, fast, hard braking) when her friend is driving, not only when you are; she doesn't ask you to let her off while her friend drives. New lines: hopping on as her friend sets off, and the driver once her friend's aboard (Jess's name Amy in hers). Bored, joining her friend weighs 0.5 against the other choices when it's possible.
- You can't get on the back with a girl there; the prompt says there's no room. Getting on the front of a quad the other girl's driving with her friend behind, the driver gets off instead of moving back.
- Tested headless: Amy driving the red, Jess asked onto it: Amy parked for her, set off once she was on ("Okay, I'll go gently. Ish."), Jess commented on the ride, and at the end Amy got off and Jess two seconds later.
- Also fixed: talking to one girl made both say "Hmm?". The wheel now records who it's for, and only she answers and pauses her boredom.
- Not fixed: leaving the crowded yard on a big loop she can bump the parked quads and back off several times before she finds her way out.

## Sitting on the back alone, and being offered a ride (2026-10-05)

- Request: sit pillion with nobody else there; the girls jump on and drive you about, and get off when bored without waiting to be told.
- `B` while looking at a parked, upright quad with a pillion seat and nobody on it puts you on the back (`canSitOnBack`). E still takes the front. The prompt shows `B sit on the back` when it's possible.
- `RideOffer` (`src/npc/rideOffer.js`, pure, tested) times it: you must have sat on a parked quad with nobody at the front for `offerRideWaitMin..Max` (6 to 25 s; any interruption restarts the count). Then `pickOfferer` chooses among the girls who are standing idle with nothing else on the go, weighted to the nearer (1 / (1 + distance / 10)), and she goes through the existing `giveRide` path (walk over, get on the front, start it, bigger loops). If nobody is free it tries again after the rest period.
- Why it ends by itself: a ride you ask for keeps going until you say stop, because `HerDrive.carrying` counted a passenger as a reason not to tire. For her own offer, `tires: true` turns that off, so the drive lasts `offerRideForMin..Max` (60 to 150 s, then the usual pull up, bored or scared, and get off). Rides you ask for with "Take me for a ride" are unchanged. If you get off before she's on, she gives up after `offerRideGiveUp` (25 s).
- After an offered ride, `offerRideRestMin..Max` (45 to 120 s) must pass before anyone offers again, even if you're still sat there; the rest runs while she climbs down, so it isn't reset by that. A first version reset it and Amy offered again at once.
- New line `offerRide` for each girl, said on the way over.
- Checked once headless (offer shortened to 3 to 4 s, ride to 15 s): Amy walked over, got on, drove, pulled up and got off with you still on the back, and didn't offer again. Not checked: Jess offering, both girls busy, real-length rides, or the new lines recorded.
- Follow-up: on the back with nobody else on the quad, E did nothing (it only opened the ride wheel when a girl was on it). It now gets you off, and the prompt says so ("Get off", with C look back). The interaction prompt is now a main line (what E does) over a row of key chips (K Ignition, R Start, Q Z Choke, F Fuel tap, B Sit on the back, X Winch cable) instead of one long line of text; prompts that are plain text are unchanged. Checked once headless with a screenshot.

- Follow-up: the rest after an offered ride comes from the girl who gave it. Jess, cheekier and bored sooner, rests 25 to 70 s (Amy 45 to 120) and drives you for 45 to 110 s (Amy 60 to 150). The wait before a first offer is still shared (Amy's numbers).

## Pillion on the orange (2026-10-05)

- The orange race quad is no longer a single-seater. Like the yellow, it gets a slim pad (`pillionSeat`) at the tail of its long seat with a grab strap and the two grip points, so the pillion pose (hands round your waist in front, on the strap when it's moving) works unchanged. Its seat is lower and thinner than the yellow's, so the pad and the `pillionAnchor` sit 4 cm lower (0.94 m against 0.98 m), the same 0.52 m behind the rider.
- Nothing else needed changing: every `hasPillion` check reads the anchor, and `B`, her offers and her getting on all follow it. Checked once in headless Chrome: she sat on the back of the orange and the pad, strap and her seat looked right from the side and behind. Not checked: a full ride on it, or you riding on its back.


## Two girls and you on one quad: the front rack (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: the second girl sits on the front rack (not squeezed onto the pillion, not standing on the back); only quads with a front rack (red, blue, green) allow it; only the girls take it, so it happens with you on the back and one girl driving (you driving with two girls aboard is left for later); the handling effect is small; it starts the same ways as the pillion swap (from her wheel, or a bored girl hopping on as the driver sets off, never onto a moving quad by choice).

- A third seat, `rack`, beside `rider` and `pillion`. The model gets a `rackAnchor` on the front rack (`quad.hasRack`, `quad.rackRider`), so which quads have one follows the body style rather than a flag in the spec. `Companion.seatKind` ('pillion' or 'rack') sets which anchor she walks to, sits on and claims, and the seat claims already used by the two-girl sharing keep her out of the others' way.
- Rule in `src/npc/rackSeat.js` (pure, tested): a rack, upright, free, her friend at the controls and not following you or waiting to give you a ride, and the back already taken (otherwise she'd take the back). The driver treats her like a friend on the back: pulls up while she's coming and sets off once she's on. With you already aboard the loops aren't scaled up a second time.
- She sits facing forward, hips 0.66 m up the deck, legs 30% of the pillion spread (`rackLegSpread`), no hold-on pose (the sitting clip's hands on her lap). `rackSeatRaise` and `rackSeatForward` tune her position. She gets off when her friend has stopped driving (the same rule as the back), landing beside the front wing. She can't be asked to drive from the rack: she'd have to get off first.
- Handling: with a girl on the rack the steering lock is `rackSteerLoss` (0.08) smaller. Nothing else changes: weight isn't modelled for riders anywhere, so there's no nose dip or extra load on the front springs.
- Checked once in headless Chrome: Amy gave me a ride on the green, Jess was asked to sit on the front, waited for the quad to stop, climbed on and sat upright on the rack with Amy driving on. Not checked: getting off, a full ride with her aboard, the other two racked quads by eye, and the swap ("Let me drive") with her on the rack. She says the usual "get on" acknowledgement; no new lines.


## Kind words (2026-10-05)

- Two new voice situations, `loveYou` and `careForYou`, with their own lines for Amy and for Jess (hers are gruffer and cheekier but still meant). The shared `nearbyWarm` already had the odd "I love you" among its lines; these are separate so the sincere ones can be rarer and have a long cooldown (240 s and 180 s).
- When: `loveYou` on a smooth ride with you (fun or gentle for 15 s, 3% a second, which is the slow check) or after 15 s stood together on foot (5%); `careForYou` instead of it when it's raining, on foot or riding with you after 40 s. Both go through the Chatter, so they wait their turn and never talk over the other girl.
- No scorekeeping yet: how often she says it doesn't depend on how you treat her. That is item 8's job (who you favour), which can turn these chances up or down.
- Checked: unit test that both girls have lines for both and none says he, she or they. Not checked in the running game: how often they come up in practice.

## No HUD on the back, and a check on girls getting off (2026-10-05)

- Sitting on the back, the crosshair and the action prompt ("Talk to Amy", "C Look back") are hidden. Captions and toasts stay, since they are what the girls say. E and C still work and the help panel (H) lists them. The prompt comes back as soon as the menu opens or you get off.
- Checked, by reading the code and a new test, that a girl who has been following you gets off when bored. You get off your quad: after 30 to 90 s (Jess 15 to 50) she stops following, then either drives on alone or pulls up; pulling up she gets off, or (`driveBackChance`) moves onto the back by choice, where `boredOnTheBack` has her get off after a minute or two. Nothing needed fixing.

## The polo collar, third go

- Feedback: the collar sat lower than the top of the blue shirt and looked separate; it should start where the neck skin stops. A real folded collar wanted, and her chest was hiding part of the placket.
- Cause: the band was placed from the neck bone (y 1.20 m), but the shirt's neckline is about 4 cm higher up the middle of the neck. `shirtVertices` in `src/npc/outfit.js` now reads which body vertices are shirt from the recoloured texture, and the neckline is the top of those up the middle of the neck. The band is the shirt's own top 3.5 cm pushed out a few millimetres, so it joins the shirt exactly.
- The folded collar is a ring round the neckline with a profile (`collarSection`): up a 2 cm stand, over a crease, down as a flap resting on the shirt. The flap runs longer near the front opening, so it ends in points. Its radius is found by rays at each height, so it follows the shoulders and chest, and it is carried by the upper spine.
- The old flat flaps are gone. The placket is a ribbon laid along the chest surface (ten samples, lifted 7 mm along the surface normal) rather than a straight strip, so the bust no longer swallows it. Buttons sit on the ribbon, facing out along the normal.
- Colours: the collar and placket use the shirt's exact colour (the texture holds the linear value as if sRGB, so the material colour is converted the same way), then are lightened by 1.6 and 1.9 so the folds and placket read against the dark navy and burgundy.
- Checked once headless on Amy, close-up from the front and side. Not checked: Jess's burgundy by eye, and the collar while she rides or sits.
- Placket, follow-up (it still clipped her neck): the neck rises almost vertically from the neckline, so a ribbon laid by raycast from the neckline ran up the neck itself. It now starts 3 cm below the front neckline and is 12 cm long. Checked headless from the front and the side on Amy.
- Collar, fourth go (it read as a mandarin collar, and the front points were small): the stand is lower (1.2 cm, was 2 cm), the flap wider (3.2 cm, was 1.4 cm) with a clear crease, and the flap is lighter than the stand (vertex colours, `COLLAR_SHADE`) so the fold shows. The front opening is narrower and the flap runs 3.6 cm longer at its ends, so the points hang down either side of the placket. Checked headless on Amy, front and side; Jess not looked at.

## Who you favour (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: the score counts rides, talking, her favourite quad, washing it, and being ignored, and fades back to nothing; the player never sees a number, but the tuning panel shows it; the consequences are voice lines, ride willingness, banter and a neglected girl taking a quad off alone; a neglected girl sometimes refuses; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/favour.js` (pure, tested). One score per girl. Events: she drives you about (+0.05 a second), you open her wheel (+1), you get on her favourite quad (+1.5; the quad is the `favourite` each girl already picks at random for her "I like the red" remarks), you hose it (+0.1 a second), you talk to her friend with her stood there (-1), she's on foot while her friend drives you (-0.02 a second). Scores fade by 0.01 a second (a score of 10 loses about 6 a minute) and stop at plus or minus 20.
- What matters is her standing against her friend, `(her score - friend's) / 6`, clamped to -1..1. Beyond plus or minus 0.35 she's favoured or neglected. With two girls it is symmetric, so treating both the same keeps both even. Everything else reads that one number.
- Favoured: kind words (`loveYou`, `careForYou`) come up to twice as often and she has her own `favouredWarm` lines when stood near you; she's weighted up to 1.7 times in who offers you a ride. Neglected: the kind words fade to nothing, `neglectedSulk` lines instead, and ride offers weight down to 0.3.
- Refusing: only a neglected girl, up to 35% at the worst, and only for requests about a quad (ride, follow, drive, start, get on). It only happens when her friend is standing about and could do the same thing, so you're never left without: the friend does it two seconds later, gloating 70% of the time (otherwise her usual okay). If the friend can't take it on, she just does it.
- Bored and neglected, her chance of choosing to drive is multiplied up to 2.5 times, and when she does she shouts that she's off on her own (`sulkOff`).
- The rivalry: a new banter kind `favoured` (four exchanges each way, one every 90 s at the soonest) between the two when they're stood about near you and one is clearly favoured.
- The tuning panel (backtick) has a "Favour (live)" folder with each girl's score, standing and mood, plus buttons to push either one by 5 or reset, and a "Favour" folder with every number above.
- Checked once headless (banter timing shortened, refusals made certain): with Amy favoured the three-line exchange played, and asking the neglected Jess for a ride got "Make me.", then Amy walked over, gloated and drove. The ten new situations are recorded for both girls. Not checked: how quickly the standing builds up in an ordinary play session (the numbers are a first guess), Jess being the favoured one in the running game, or a neglected girl's solo drive by eye.


## Pranks (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: only while you're away; you find out by the quad misbehaving or the thing missing, no hint; hidden things go to fixed spots and come back by themselves; Jess does engines, Amy hides things; favour changes who does it; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/pranks.js` (pure, tested). The first prank can't happen until 4 minutes in, then at most one every 10 minutes. Every 5 s there is a 12% chance of a look for something fair game: a quad that is parked, switched off, not being got on and more than 30 m from you (Jess: tap not already off, or choke off on an engine warmer than half), or the jerry can or toolbox not in your hands and more than 30 m from you (Amy). You must not be riding for the item pranks.
- Favour: a favoured girl never pranks; a neglected one is twice as likely to be the one.
- The girl has to be stood about. She walks over (`sneakTo` in `companion.js`, a `pranking` state), pauses 1.4 s, then does it, and only if it is still fair game then (nobody on the quad, not running, item not picked up). Otherwise it lapses; so does one she hasn't reached in 90 s.
- Hiding: the item moves to one of the farm's existing can spots (`jerryCanSpots`, so known good ground) between 8 and 40 m from where it was and not within 12 m of you. She puts it back herself after 10 minutes. Picking it up ends the prank.
- Finding out: sat on a pranked quad for 3 s, or putting it right yourself, or walking within 6 m of where a hidden item should be. The culprit then says a `prankGiggle` line (not a "tell"; Amy's poker face is poor, Jess blames you). Talk to her afterwards and she says `prankConfess` once. Both are new situations for both girls, recorded.
- Tap off gives the existing 10 to 30 s warm idle before it dies; choke on a warm engine runs rich and may stall. No new engine behaviour.
- The tuning panel (backtick) has a "Pranks" folder with every number above.
- Checked once headless, with the delays and distances squeezed: Jess and Amy both walked over, paused and did it (choke on the orange, toolbox hidden with its home remembered), no errors. Not checked: how it feels at the real pace, the culprit's lines in the running game, a hidden item being found and put back by eye.

## Teaching them (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: coaching is a "Coach her" slice on the wheel you get on the back while she drives, listing only what fits the moment; four skills (starting, choke, cornering, mud) replace the random flaws; right-moment advice teaches, practice teaches far more slowly, wrong-moment or repeated advice doesn't; her lines show her level; only from the back for now; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/skills.js` (pure, tested). A level 0 to 1 for each skill per girl. 0.5 behaves exactly as she did before; `mistake(level)` is 2 at 0, 1 at 0.5 and 0 at 1 and scales her chance of each error. Amy starts at starting 0.5, choke 0.3, cornering 0.6, mud 0.35; Jess at 0.45, 0.3, 0.4, 0.65.
- What each skill changes in `HerDrive`: choke is the chance she forgets it (`driveForgetChokeChance` is now the chance at 0.5, so a start of 0.3 forgets about 0.63 of the time); starting scales how often she checks the tap and puts the choke on and how soon she remembers after a failed go; cornering scales how often she yanks the bars and the cornering speed (0.8 to 1.2 times `driveCornerAccel`); mud gives a better-judged boost when stuck and one more go before giving up from 0.8. The levels are a live object, so coaching changes them under her mid-drive.
- The right moment (`HerDrive.isMoment`): choke is running, warm (over half) with the choke still on; starting is cranking, just stalled or a stall in the last 5 s; cornering is a yank in the last 5 s or the bars well over in a turn; mud is a rollback or being stuck in the last 5 s, stuck over a second or sinking. The wheel only shows advice that fits when it opens, and the check is made again when you pick it.
- Learning: advice at the right moment gives +0.12 and +0.5 favour; advice on the same skill within 25 s is nagging (nothing, -0.5 favour, she's short with you); the wrong moment gives nothing. Practice gives +0.01, at most once every 30 s per skill, for a clean corner, the choke taken off when warm, a start that catches or getting out of a bog. A good piece of advice also makes her act at once (choke off, a boost in the mud). Opening her wheel already counts as talking to her for favour.
- Her voice: for each skill, `skill_<skill>_novice` (she's just made the mistake and is under 0.35, said in place of the usual yelp), `_learning` and `_learned` (on a clean success, half the time), `taught_<skill>` after good advice, and `coachWrongTime` and `coachNagged`. Lines are in `src/npc/skillLines.js`, Jess's with her own wording. 18 new situations each.
- Checked once headless with Jess driving a ride: the Coach options appeared (choke off, corners, mud), advice raised her choke from 0.3 to 0.42 and she said the taught line, the same advice at once got "I heard you the first time!", and she had said her novice choke line earlier. Not checked: how it feels over a long session (the 0.12 step means about five good pieces of advice from novice to learned, which is a first guess), Amy, the other three skills in the running game, or the wheel by eye.

## Favours (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: three simple favours (wash her favourite quad, take her to the bales, fetch the jerry can; a race against Jess is left for its own item); she asks only while you're on foot near her and she's idle; nothing shows on screen and she reminds you if you talk to her; doing it is a big favour score, ignoring it lapses into a grudge; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/requests.js` (pure, tested). First ask not before 180 s, then each girl no more than every 240 s, one open request per girl. Every 5 s a 15% chance that someone asks, among the girls who are idle, within 14 m of you, with you on foot and a favour that makes sense: wash needs her favourite quad (the one she already likes for her remarks) over 30% muddy; bales needs a quad with a free pillion seat; the can needs it more than 6 m from her.
- Done: wash when that quad is under 10% muddy; bales when she's on the back of the quad you're riding, it's under 0.8 m/s and within 7 m of a straw bale (the spots are now `strawBaleSpots` in `layout.js`, filtered the same way `props.js` filters them, so nothing points at a bale that wasn't built); the can when it's within 3 m of her, with you also within 3 m if you're still holding it. Done gives +4 favour and her thanks; a favoured girl with her friend within 12 m gloats 4 s later 60% of the time.
- Lapsed: after 360 s she stops waiting, -2 favour, and holds a grudge for 600 s. A grudge adds a 15% chance of saying no to quad requests (on top of the neglected-girl refusals, using the larger of the two; the friend takes it on as before) and she says a grudge line the first time you open her wheel. Doing any favour for her clears it. While one's open, opening her wheel gets a reminder line instead.
- Lines: `ask`, `thanks`, `remind` and `grudge` for each of Wash, Bales and Can, plus `gloatFavour`, in `src/npc/requestLines.js` (Jess's with her own wording). 15 new situations each.
- Bales follow-up (TODO 5): the girl who asked didn't do anything when you got on a quad, so the favour mostly lapsed. Now `Requests.joiner`: 1.5 s after you get on a quad with a free pillion seat (`joinDelay`, so you see her coming), if she's still waiting, free and within 30 m (`joinRange`), she walks over and gets on the back (`requestMount`) and says her reminder line. Once per quad; get on another and she tries again. Not checked in the running game: how it looks when you drive off before she's aboard (she chases the quad's mount point), or the timing by feel.
- Checked once headless (delays squeezed): both girls asked, the can was put by Amy, she thanked and her favour went up. Not checked: a bale favour or a wash by eye, the grudge and reminder lines in the running game, a lapse at the real six-minute pace, or how the 4-minute pace feels over a session.

## Places to be (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: sitting on the straw bales and the paddock fence in the sun, standing at the barn doors in the rain, all at fixed spots; lying in the grass dropped for now (no lying pose in the rig); it's still her own idea (a new bored choice, and rain sending them in); she leaves at once when you talk to her; the two can share a kind of place but not a spot; they bicker as usual when near each other there.

- `src/npc/places.js` (pure, tested): `isFine` (sunny or sunny intervals and under 0.05 rain), `nearestSpot`, `restStay` (60 to 180 s), and `Places`, which watches the rain. Over 0.25 rain, a girl who's stood about or sat on a sunny spot waits a random 4 to 18 s then is sent to the nearest free shelter spot (anywhere on the farm); under 0.08 rain a girl at the barn waits 15 to 45 s then comes out. A girl on a quad or doing something else is left alone. Spots are `restSpots` in `layout.js` (four fence seats, three barn door spots) plus one for each straw bale not too near the track, which the game adds. Spots taken, or promised to a girl who's still getting up, aren't offered to her friend.
- She sits with the sitting pose, hips on the spot (bale top 1.38 m, paddock rail 1.05 m), climbing up from behind the seat over about `mountDuration` and down the same way; at the barn she just stands facing out. `companionResting.js` has the `resting` state and `goRest`, `leaveRest` and `currentSpot`. Her collider is off while she's sat so she doesn't push the bale.
- Bored choice `rest`: `boredRestChance` 0.35, only on a fine day with a free bale or fence seat within 45 m. A girl sat out is not "idle" for the other systems, except that she counts as stood about for banter, can be asked to drive, and can be picked to offer you a ride (she gets down first).
- Lines: `restGo`, `restSat`, `restRain`, `restShelter` and `restLeave` for each girl (`placeLines.js`).
- Checked once headless: Amy sat on the fence rail and Jess on a bale (screenshots looked right: hips on the rail, legs hanging, boots clear of the ground; on the bale her legs go slightly into it), then with rain forced both walked to the barn doors and stood against them. A first run had Jess, 82 m away, ignore the rain because the range was 80, and both girls pick the same doorway spot while one was still getting up; both fixed. Not checked: the climb up and down by eye, the girls leaving when talked to, the real-length rain delay, Jess's sitting lines in the running game, or a girl run into by a quad while she's sat.

## Rev-offs (TODO 2)

- `src/npc/revOff.js` (pure, tested). A blip is your throttle rising past `blipAt` 0.5 while you are on a running quad going under `maxYourSpeed` 2 m/s. Any girl within `hearRange` 16 m who is sat on a running quad with nobody at its controls may answer: Jess always (`jessReplyChance` 1), Amy `amyReplyChance` 0.2. She waits 0.3 to 0.8 s, then holds her quad's `helperThrottle` (the one the starting knack uses) for `blipHold` 0.35 s.
- Escalation: blipping again within `roundWindow` 7 s of her answer ending is another round. Round n is n blips (at most 4), each held `holdStep` 0.12 s longer (at most 1.1 s). Amy gives up after `amyMaxRound` 1 and says sorry. A blip while she is still answering is ignored; a quiet spell resets the rounds.
- Complaints: from `complainRound` 3, each answer has a `complainChance` 0.6 of being the last, at most one a minute. Amy tells you both off if she is within 40 m and not the one revving; otherwise the engine complains: stalls if under 0.5 warm or over 0.4 worn (Jess says so), else nothing more than the noise. The exchange then starts again.
- Hard revving is the existing engine model, so a cold or worn quad she keeps revving wears, and shows up in the wear tally from TODO 1 as hers.
- Lines in `revOffLines.js` (situations `revAnswer`, `revAnswerMore`, `revComplain`, `revStall`); tunables in the "Rev-offs" tuning folder.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads without errors. Not checked: a rev-off in play (the sound of two quads revving, how the girl's throttle looks on a stood quad, how often the stall comes), or the lines in play.

## Nicknames, whose quad, and hearing one (TODO 9, 10, 11)

- Recorded lines can't take a name at runtime, so every line that names a quad is written out once for the colour and once for each possible nickname (`expandQuadLines` in `quadNames.js`), keyed `kind:spec:nickname`, and the game says the one for this game's names. Two nicknames per quad (seven quads), picked per game from a seeded stream. That is a lot of lines to record (about 400 across both voices) but they are cached and the unused ones cost nothing in play.
- Nicknames (10): from `firstNameAfter` 25 s Jess names the nearest un-named quad every `nameEvery` 40 s, while she is free and you are within `nameRange` 30 m. Amy answers `nameAmy` ("I am not calling it that"). `QuadNames.refer` says what a girl calls a quad when she mentions it: Jess uses the name once it's been said; Amy keeps to the colour for `amyHolds` 4 mentions that she actually said, then the next one slips (`voice.slipped`) and Jess says `nameCaved` a couple of seconds later. After that Amy uses names too. A mention that wasn't said (cooldown, chance) doesn't count.
- Not done: the old colour-named lines ("I love the red one", the "like" and "prefer" lines) still say the colour. Names are in the new lines only: claims, hearing, and the naming itself.
- My quad (11): `Ownership.assign` gives each girl a different quad from those she can ride and has a "like" line for, and that is also set as her `favourite`, so what she says she likes best is the one she says is hers. `claim` is said once, after `claimAfter` 20 s, when you are on foot within `claimRange` 9 m of her and her quad and she is free. Objecting: someone else has been at the controls of her quad for `upsetDelay` 1.5 s and she is within `noticeRange` 35 m, at most every `upsetCooldown` 80 s; lines differ for you, Jess and Amy (`ownerUpset_you` / `_jess` / `_amy`). Only the driver counts, not somebody on the back.
- Borrowing: when Jess picks "drive" on her own, `borrowChance` 0.5 she takes Amy's quad if it is among the ones she could drive, says `borrow`, and 0.7 of the time teases after Amy objects. Amy never borrows Jess's (0). A girl wanting a drive goes for her claimed quad first, like the kids' quad for Jess.
- Hearing (9): a free girl, `chance` 0.04 a second, places a running quad her friend is driving that has been going 3 s and is 28 to 130 m away, at most every 75 s. Its sound is `rough` if it is cold (under 0.45) or worn (over 0.4), else `hard` over 7 m/s or 0.8 throttle, else `steady`. Out of sight is judged by distance only, not by what is in the way. She doesn't place you riding: you would not be near enough to hear her say it.
- Not checked in the running game: how the lines sound or feel in play, how often Jess borrows, or whether 28 m really is out of sight on this farm. Unit tests cover the logic and that every line exists.

## Warming their hands (TODO 5)

- A cold day is weather warmth under `coldBelow` 0.5 (overcast 0.48, fog 0.23, drizzle 0.19, rain 0.05 on the existing scale; sunny intervals 0.73 and sunny 1 aren't) or rain over `wetAbove` 0.25. After `startAfter` 20 s, every 2 s a free girl with a quad that's running, unattended and not moving within `warmRange` 16 m goes to it with `warmChance` 0.2, or 0.7 if her friend is already there. At most two at one engine, one each side. She stays `warmForMin` 25 to `warmForMax` 70 s, then doesn't think of it again for a minute.
- She stays in the idle state with a flag (`warmingAt`), rather than getting a state of her own, so the wheel, boredom and requests all treat her as stood about; whatever she does next (the idle state's `exit`) puts her hands down. Boredom doesn't build up while she's at it. A rig poser puts both hands over the engine with the same arm IK as the grips.
- Switching it off, or anything stopping it, while she is there: she leaves and complains (`warmOff`, Jess "Oi! I was using that!"). The engine stopping on her before she gets there counts the same.
- Not checked in the running game: where the hands land on each quad's body (the target is a fixed point over the middle of the quad, 0.78 m up), how the walk-up looks, or how often it happens on a cold day. Unit tests cover the decisions and the lines.

## Hand on the throttle (TODO 6)

- `src/npc/throttleGrab.js` (pure, tested). You are at the controls of a running quad between `minSpeed` 2 and `maxSpeed` 11 m/s with a girl on the pillion seat (not the front rack); after `firstAfter` 25 s of that she has `chance` 0.03 a second times her boldness (Jess 1, Amy 0.25) to grab. One at a time, at least `gap` 40 s after the last. Amy does it `amyMax` 1 time a game; Jess has no limit.
- The grab: her hand takes `reachTime` 0.7 s to reach your throttle grip, then the throttle rises to `throttle` 0.9 over 0.35 s and is held 1.2 to 2.4 s, then released over 0.6 s. If she gets off, or you stop riding, it ends at once. It is added to the quad as `quad.handThrottle` and taken as the larger of that and your own throttle, so your W is still there if you want more; it's ignored while you're braking or reversing.
- The arm: the pillion pose's right arm is pulled towards the quad's right grip (`gripRight`, the throttle side) as `throttleReach` rises, so she is seen reaching round you. The hand isn't posed on the grip itself, only brought to it.
- Speech: she says `grabGo` as she starts (Jess "Faster!", Amy "Oh, go on, faster!"); at the end Amy always says `grabSorry` and Jess does 60% of the time, pleased with herself.
- Not checked in the running game: how the arm looks reaching across, how 0.9 throttle feels from a stop-start pace, or whether 0.03 a second is too often for Jess. Unit tests cover the timeline, limits and lines.

## Smoke and smell (TODO 7)

- "Orange or yellow" is any two-stroke (`engine.params.strokes === 2`), which is just those two. How much a two-stroke smokes (`smokeLevel`, 0..1): 0.35 running, plus 0.5 of the throttle, plus up to 0.3 when cold; she reacts at 0.3 or more, so any running two-stroke counts.
- The plume (`inPlume`): from the pipe, 1.1 m behind the quad's middle, downwind for `plumeLength` 9 m and `plumeWidth` 1.3 m across at the pipe, widening 0.22 m per metre. Wind is the smoke's own (`windX`, `windZ`, which the weather already scales); under `calmWind` 0.35 m/s the smoke hangs straight behind the quad instead.
- A free girl (stood about) in it for `reactAfter` 1.2 s reacts: Amy coughs, waves her right hand across her face for 2.6 s (a rig poser, `companionWaving.js`) and says `smokeCough`, at most every 30 s. Jess says `smokeLove`, at most every 50 s. A girl on a quad or walking doesn't react.
- Following you (`followSmokeGap`, a multiple of `followGap` 8 m): Amy 1.8, so about 14 m behind a two-stroke; Jess 0.7, about 5.6 m. Each says `followSmoke` once per drive when she first notices she is behind one. It also shifts the point at which she pulls up when you stop.
- Not checked in the running game: how the wave looks, whether a plume 9 m long matches what you see in the smoke particles, or whether Amy's 14 m gap loses you on a fast ride. Unit tests cover the plume shape, the cough and the gap.

## Racing (TODO 3)

- Agreed with you: use the mud track, with non-collision cones; feed the rivalry and track wins and losses.
- `src/npc/race.js` (pure, tested) runs the race; `HerDrive` has a race mode. After `startAfter` 120 s, with both girls free for `freeFor` 20 s, `chance` 0.015 a second (at most one race per `cooldown` 300 s): Jess dares Amy 0.7 of the time. Each girl's quad is her claimed one if it's free (`pickQuads`), else any she may ride that is parked and unattended. Neither can be the one you're on.
- Each girl is sent to drive her quad with a race: `route` (the grid point, then one lap round the track and back to the grid point, which is the finish), a cruise speed (`cruiseBase` 9 + `cruiseConfidence` 4 times her driving confidence, plus or minus 1.5, so Jess is usually quicker; the quad's power does the rest), full throttle allowed and no fright speed or pauses. She starts it as usual, drives to the grid and stops; when both are there it counts down `countdown` 3 s (`raceCount`), then she goes. First over the line wins; the other has `raceLose` after a pause. Out of it (a quad that stalls out, a bog that holds it, you get on) is a loss; `raceTimeout` 150 s after the start ends it for anyone still going; `gatherTimeout` 150 s without both on the grid cancels it (they stop where they are).
- The route (`planLap`): a waypoint every `routeSpacing` 6 m round the track, each girl 0.9 m to her side of the middle so they don't share a line. Every bog and puddle is avoided sideways, on whichever side she's on, to its radius plus `hazardMargin` 1.4 m (puddles count as 1.5 m wider than they are). That is needed: a puddle at 12 m/s drowns the engine (checked: in the first headless runs both girls stalled at the puddle by the yard, each with water in the engine, and neither could restart in time). The waypoint arrival radius is also much tighter in a race (0.2 of her speed in metres, not 0.5), or she cuts the corner round a puddle.
- Score: wins and losses by girl are kept in `race.tally` for the game. `race.rivalry()` is the one ahead by `rivalLead` 2 wins or more. After a race there is a banter exchange for the winner and loser (`raced`, six, three for each result), and while one is ahead and both are stood about close together, a `rivals` exchange now and then (four). Not tied into the favour system.
- The cones are four, either side of the track at the point nearest the yard (`src/world/startLine.js`), no collider.
- Checked headless (five short runs, forced to start at once): they gather and grid up, the count-down runs, both leave the line together and Jess ran a whole lap in about 75 s and pulled up over the line. The puddle problem above was found and fixed that way. Not seen: Amy finishing (the run was cut at 150 s), the result and win lines in play, the banter after, the cones, or a race with the green or another stubborn starter. A girl who can't start the quad she picked cancels the race; that is common with a cold green and with novices (two of the five runs), which I haven't tried to cure.

## Racing them (TODO 1)

- Agreed with you: three ways in (Jess dares you, `Enter` at the line, or they invite you when they start one), the girls stage so they face the right way, you are judged on checkpoints, the tally gets a third name, no tie to the favour system.
- You are a racer called `you` in `Race` (`src/npc/race.js`, pure, tested). `update` takes `you: { riding, near, still }`. Near is within `nearLine` 45 m of the line; a dare needs you sat still, engine running, for `dareStill` 4 s, then `dareChance` 0.05 a second when the girls are free and the usual timing is up; Jess is the one who dares. An ordinary girls' race invites you if you are riding and near. Your quad is `picks.you`; the girls are never given it.
- Grid: you in the middle, the girls `gridSpacing` 3 m either side (1.5 as before when you are not in). You count as lined up (`youOnGrid`) within `youGridRadius` 5 m of your grid point, stopped and within `youGridYaw` 0.7 rad of the track's direction; once lined up you may creep up to 2 m/s on a slope. If you haven't lined up in `youWait` 30 s, get off, or pull off the line in the count-down, you are dropped (`youDropped`) and they race without you rather than calling it off.
- `YouRun` judges your lap: the lap is `youCheckpoints` 4 bands in order, so the only way to finish is to go round (cutting across or going backwards gets you nowhere), and the finish is within `youFinishRadius` 8 m of the line after the last band. Off the quad or stalled for `youOutAfter` 15 s and you are out. The wins and losses use the same tally; `rivalry()` ignores `you`, so the score they go on about is still Amy against Jess.
- Facing the right way: the girls' route now starts with a staging point 14 m behind their grid point on the track (`RACE_STAGE_BACK`), so they come at the grid along the track. If one still arrives more than `raceGridYaw` 0.6 rad off the track's direction (in the Companion folder) she goes back to the staging point and tries again, twice at most, then accepts it.
- Lines: `raceInviteYou` and `raceJoinYou` (the inviter, then the other), `raceBeatYou` (the winner, when you were in), `raceLoseToYou` (a girl who finishes behind you). When you win, the girls' own post-race banter is skipped.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless load with a forced girls' race (they pick quads and the route builds, no errors). Not checked in play: a race with you on the grid, the toasts, whether the staging point sits clear of everything, or the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

## A girl driving that keeps reversing

- Jamie: sometimes one of them driving gets stuck going backwards all the time. Not reproduced, so this is the loop found by reading `herDriving.js`, not a confirmed cause: she meets something in front of her (or the `stuck` check finds it within 3.5 m), reverses for 1.5 to 2.5 s, pauses, drives, meets the same thing again, and so on for ever. Each reversal also skipped a waypoint, so even a new loop was planned from the same blocked spot facing it.
- Fix: `backOff` counts backing off in the same spot (within `backOffRadius` 6 m and `backOffWithin` 25 s of the last time). The second time she throws away her waypoints, so the next plan tries other headings and picks one that is clear; the fifth (`backOffsToGiveUp`) she says there's no room (`noRoom`, the existing line), stops and gets off. Moving elsewhere or waiting longer starts the count again. In a race the route is kept and only the give-up applies; following you, the point to aim at is recalculated anyway. All three are in the Companion folder.
- If it still happens, the next suspect is the quad's reverse flag, which only flips when it's moving slower than 0.3 m/s, so a quad rolling back on a slope can stay in reverse while she gives it forward throttle. I didn't change that.

## Drag race (TODO 2)

- Agreed with you: a flat gravel strip for now (tarmac comes with the bigger field); you against one girl; `Enter` at the start line to ask; Jess's challenge after you have sat still on a quad for 10 s; the lap race's behaviour left as it was; Jess and Amy both have lines. Then you asked for it rougher: ragged edges and ends that thin into the grass, and only cones at the start and finish (no banner, no painted lines).
- The strip (`layout.dragStrip`, `world/dragStrip.js`): 56 m from start to finish line, 8 m of run-off, 10 m wide, heading a little south of east. That is as long as the room allows: the track, the bog at the north-east, the bales near (12, 14) and the jump's run-up and landing leave about 56 m clear in the field. A test checks it is clear of the track (by more than the dish's reach), bogs, puddles, bales, the ramp and the hedges, and level. The ground under it is levelled (`terrainHeight.js`) to the average of the ground along it, with a 6 m shoulder. Not done: gravel grip or squeal (the surface is a mesh over the grass), which belongs with the tarmac in the bigger field.
- `src/npc/drag.js` (pure, tested) runs it, separate from `Race` so the lap race is untouched. It shares `pickQuads`, and the girl's drive is the lap race's: HerDrive's race mode with a three-point route (staging point 10 m behind the line, her grid point, a point past the finish), plus two small additions: `blip()` on the race (she holds the brake and opens the throttle on the spot) and `reroute(race)` (Jess is already on her quad beside you, and is sent to the strip without getting off).
- The challenge: you sit still on a quad with the engine running for `blipStill` 10 s (after `startAfter` 90 s, `cooldown` 240 s, both girls free, no lap race on); then `blipChance` 0.03 a second Jess drives to 3.2 m beside you (`approachTimeout` 40 s, forgotten if you get off or ride off). She waits `blipPause` 1.2 s, then blips twice. Your answer is your throttle going from under 0.15 to over 0.6 while still; a throttle already open when she arrived doesn't count. You have `blipWait` 8 s after her last blip, then she calls you chicken and it's off.
- The race: she takes the lane you are not nearer to (2 m either side of the middle); you are lined up within `gridRadius` 2.5 m of your point, stopped and within `gridYaw` 0.5 rad of the strip. Count-down 3 s. Rolling more than `falseStartAlong` 1 m over the line is a false start: she says so, and you line up again (`falseStarts` 2 forgiven, the next calls it off). Pulling off the grid some other way calls it off. Finish is along the strip reaching its length, for both of you (not her arrival at a waypoint, so it's fair). A stalled or abandoned quad is out for `youOutAfter` 8 s; `raceTimeout` 45 s. Wins and losses go in `drag.tally`, not the lap race's score.
- The gravel mesh sits 4 to 7 cm above the ground everywhere (its edges used to dip below the visual terrain, which is coarser than `heightAt`, so they shimmered as you moved), and no grass tufts are placed within 3 m of the surface (`props.js`). The ragged edges are fixed at build time from a seeded generator.
- Jamie: the girls still struggled to reach the line and face along it. Both races now ease a girl who has arrived and is holding on the grid onto her exact point and round to face along the track or strip, at 2.5 times a second's worth of the gap (`Game.helpOnGrid`), instead of sending her back to the staging point to come at it again (the `gridYaw` retry is not used any more; the races pass `assistYaw`). Only within 5 m of the point and below 1.5 m/s, and only until the start. Not checked in play: how the nudge looks, or whether a quad nosing a hedge gets pulled through it.
- Jamie: they needn't get off when it's over. Each girl, in both the lap race and the drag race, rolls `keepRidingChance` 0.5 (Racing folder) when her race is set up. If she keeps riding, crossing the finish drops her back to her ordinary driving (`HerDrive.carryOn`): her usual throttle limit, nerves and pauses, a drive of the usual length from then, and the usual dismount at the end. Otherwise she pulls up and gets off as before. Not checked in play.
- Carrying on after the other things, too (Jamie, after the above): the same idea for doughnuts, the ramp, showing off (unless there was no room) and the end of follow the leader. When one finishes (a jump that ended in a bail still means she gets off, scared) each girl rolls `keepRidingAfter` 0.3 (Companion folder), lower than the races' 0.5 because carrying on straight after a doughnut is less natural; if she wins the roll she goes back to ordinary driving (`HerDrive.endActivity`, `carryOn`). `carryOn` restores the throttle limit, nerves, pause chance, drive length and loop size she had before the activity changed them, and drops the doughnut, ramp, show-off and follow state. A convoy that is broken up (someone got on a quad, one stopped doing anything) still just stops. Not checked in play.
- She drives flat out with the quad's power deciding the result, so the quad you pick matters. Not tied to the favour system.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless load with no errors. Not checked in play: the challenge and the race end to end, how the strip looks, whether her blip revs the engine on the brake with every quad, whether the staging point sits clear of things, or the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

## Race hardening, and calling the girls back

- Jamie: the race gather stage was slow and they mostly drove round the track looking for the start line. Cause found by reading: backing off something (the other racer on the grid 1.8 m away, a hedge on the way) skipped the waypoint she was heading for, and the start line was waypoint 0, so she carried on round the whole lap without ever lining up until the race was called off. Now in a race she has another go at the same waypoint after backing off (and never counts herself past the grid before the start); the grid point counts from 3.5 m (`raceGridRadius`, was 2.2); the two quads sit 3 m apart (+/-1.5 m, not 1.8); a racer who has stopped doing anything about it for 4 s is out, which calls the race off; and the gather timeout is 100 s, not 150. Tried first, and dropped: stacking the second quad 6 m behind the first, which put it right in the first one's way, and ignoring the other racer in the obstacle probe, which had Amy pushing into Jess.
- Checked headless: the line-up and the call-back below. Not seen again: a whole race from the new grid. That run ended with both on the grid in time but I did not run it on to the finish.
- Call the girls back (N, or "Call the girls back" in the tuning panel's Girls folder): any race is called off, and each girl is told to stop what she's doing and stand in front of the barn doors, one either side of the middle (`recallSpot`). Driving, she pulls up and gets off first; sat on a quad or the bales, she gets down first; mid-change she's nudged again every 0.7 s until she is there (within 2.5 m and stood), or two minutes have gone. Races don't start while it is going on. They say `recalled`. Once they're there they carry on as usual (wander, get bored, and so on); this is not a hold. Checked headless: both were at the doors in about 20 s.

## Reading the dash (TODO 12)

- Three things get read out, the ones the dash shows as lamps or a gauge: fuel (the fuel lamp, which comes on at the reserve), temperature (the hot lamp) and a cold engine (not a lamp, but what the cold light and gauge show). Nothing about the battery or faults, which the dash doesn't show.
- Amy reads in good time: fuel the moment the lamp comes on, hot the moment the hot lamp does, cold when the engine is under `coldBelow` 0.4 and you have been faster than `coldPace` 5 m/s or on more than 0.6 throttle for 2 s. Jess is late: fuel only at `fuelLateLevel` 0.04 of a tank, hot after the lamp has been on `hotLateFor` 6 s, and cold only once the engine has warmed past 0.65 after you had gone hard while cold for 6 s ("you thrashed it").
- Each reading is once per spell (it can be said again after the lamp goes off, or the engine warms) and per girl, with `gap` 45 s between readings by the same girl. Only the girl on the pillion seat, with you at the controls and the engine running. The dash lamps come from `quad.lamps`, kept up to date with the dash itself.
- Not checked in the running game: how often it comes up, or whether 0.04 is far enough past the red to feel "too late".

## Impatient follower (TODO 13)

- In `HerDrive.passTarget` (herDriving.js), which stands in for the normal follow target while she is passing. Stuck behind a pace between 0.8 and `overtakeBelow` 6 m/s for `overtakeAfter` 20 s, she has `overtakeChance` a second to start (Jess 0.04, Amy 0, in the Companion folder). Not in a race.
- Overtaking: she aims for a point `overtakeAhead` 12 m + 10 m ahead of you and 3.5 m to one side (a random side), at your speed plus `overtakeSpeedUp` 4 m/s (capped at her follow top speed). Once she is 12 m ahead (or after 25 s, or 70 m away) she pulls up and waits (`overtook`: "Come on, slowcoach!"). When you get within `overtakeGloatRange` 10 m she looks pleased (`waitedAhead`). When you've gone by her, or after `overtakeWait` 30 s, she is following again, from behind.
- The same pass is what a convoy uses to swap who is in front (TODO 14).
- Not checked in the running game: whether she overtakes cleanly on the real ground (the side she picks might be a hedge; her usual obstacle avoidance applies), or how the wait looks.

## Follow the leader (TODO 14)

- `src/npc/convoy.js` (pure, tested). Gating like the race: after `startAfter` 150 s, both free for `freeFor` 20 s, `chance` 0.012 a second, `cooldown` 420 s, and not while a race is on or they've been called back. Jess leads 0.6 of the time. Quads picked as in the race (`pickQuads`).
- The leader drives her usual loops, bigger (`rideLoopScale`) and without tiring (`driveFor` 1e6). The follower is an ordinary follower (the same HerDrive follow as for you: gap, smoke gap, catch-up) whose target is the leader's quad (`followWho`), so she waits for the leader to set off. When both are going it is "running".
- After `swapMin` 60 to `swapMax` 120 s the follower is told to overtake (the pass from TODO 13, `startPass`); when she is ahead and waiting, the roles flip: the old leader's follow target becomes the new leader's quad and the new leader drops her follow and plans her own loops. A pass that doesn't get there within 45 s is given up and they carry on in the same order. After `swaps` 2 swaps and a `lastLeg` 60 s they stop (`drive.stop('bored')`) and get off.
- If either drive ends, someone gets on one of the quads, or one stands about doing nothing for 4 s, the whole convoy ends and the other stops. Calling them back (N) ends it too. A convoy that hasn't got both going within `formTimeout` 120 s is called off.
- The swap is a banter exchange (`swapped`, four, two for each order), and each girl has an offer and an answer line.
- Checked headless (one run, with the timers shortened): forming, both driving, the follower overtaking, the swap, and the stop at the end all happened; Jess backed off obstacles several times in the field. Not seen: the banter, or a full-length convoy.

A girl with `boredDoughnutChance` (Jess 0.12) finds a ring that is clear of bogs, puddles, obstacles and slopes (`Companion.doughnutPlan`) and takes a quad there.

## Tea on the rack (TODO 16)

- `src/npc/tea.js` (pure, tested). After `startAfter` 90 s a free girl with no mug out has `chance` 0.01 a second (after a `cooldown` of 120 s) to go to the nearest parked, unattended quad with a rack within `range` 18 m and leave a mug on its rear rack (she walks up and pauses like for a prank, then it appears). One mug each, one per quad. The mug stays `leaveMin` 90 to `leaveMax` 180 s; then if the quad is still parked and nobody is on it, she walks over and takes it.
- It falls when that quad goes faster than `fallSpeed` 2.5 m/s or tips over, whoever is riding it. The mug drops from the rack with some of the quad's speed, lands on its side with a tea stain (60 s) and the owner says `teaFell` wherever she is (Amy "My tea! You have driven off with my tea on the back!"). Nothing warns you when you get on. It only counts the quad's speed, not bumps.
- The rear rack, not the front: the front is where she sits, and you don't see the back, which is the point. Added a `mugAnchor` to the utility quads' models (red, blue, green, loan; the sport quads and the kids' quad have no rack).
- Checked headless: Amy left a mug on the blue, it was on the rack, and pushing the quad off made it fall with a stain. Not seen: the walk up and collecting it, or how the mug looks on the rack by eye.

## Talking to the quad (TODO 2)

- Three new situations about a quad, written out for the colour and each nickname like the claims (`quadLines.js`): `startPat` and `startCheer` (Jess only) and `startSorry` (Amy only). Jess says `startPat` on her first crank of an attempt (70%) and `startCheer` when it catches (70%, else the old `startCaught`). Amy says `startSorry` (80%) when `StartingAttempt` raises the new `flooded` event. Both go through `sayQuad`, so Jess uses the nickname once it has been given and Amy holds to the colour until she slips.
- Flooded means plug fouling over `floodedFouling` 0.4 or richness over `floodedRich` 0.2 (the same thresholds the crank hint uses), judged after a failed go, once per attempt. Those two are in `DEFAULT_STARTING_PARAMS`; that set has no tuning ranges, so they are not in the panel.
- Not done: the pat. Jess says it but there is no tank-patting animation. Not checked in the running game: how the lines sound in play, or how often a girl floods one. The game loads without errors; unit tests cover the event and that every line exists for every quad and nickname.

## A head start on your usual quad (TODO 3)

- Your usual quad is the one you've spent most seconds at the controls of, once that's at least `usualAfter` 30 s (`src/npc/headStart.js`, pure, tested). It only counts you riding, not sitting on the back.
- The trigger is you on foot, on a cold day (the same test as warming their hands), that quad off and unattended, you between `minRange` 5 m and `noticeRange` 24 m of it and closing at over `closeSpeed` 0.8 m/s (smoothed). It is rolled once per approach and again only after you've walked off beyond 1.5 times the notice range. Nothing before `startAfter` 90 s.
- Favoured girl (the existing mood from favour.js): if she's free, within `girlRange` 40 m and no further from the quad than you are (plus 2 m), she goes with an 85% chance and runs the ordinary starting routine, so she can flood it or give up like any other go. Neglected girl: with a 70% chance she starts her own quad (the one she claimed) instead, if that's off and free, and says so pointedly. Girls who are even with you do nothing. Each girl waits `cooldown` 240 s before doing either again.
- Lines: `headStartGo` and `snubGo` for each girl (`headStartLines.js`). When she gets it running, the existing `startWarm` line is her handing it over.
- Not checked in the running game: how often it fires in a real game or how the lines sound in play. Checked once headless with the approach faked: a favoured Amy walked to the usual quad and had it running.

- It starts when both girls are free, within `together` 7 m of each other, you are on foot, and you have been between `hearMin` 4.5 m and `hearMax` 14 m of the nearer one for `settle` 2 s without being noticed (chance 0.4 per second after that, nothing before 60 s into the game, then `cooldown` 150 s). All in `DEFAULT_GOSSIP_PARAMS`, in the panel as "Talking about you".

Mid-exchange that cuts the speaker off (`voice.cut()`), leaves `silence` 0.9 s, and the girl who was not talking says a `gossipCaught` line. If one of them wanders off instead, it just stops.
