## Shirts the colour of her quad (TODO 5)

- A girl's favourite is the quad the ownership deal gives her at the start of a game (`ownership.assign`), so the shirt can't be fixed in `characters.js`. The game recolours it right after the deal, using the spec's `bodyColor` as it is, collar included.
- `outfit.js` keeps the untouched texture on the material (`userData.baseMap`) and `reshirt` recolours from that, then refits the garments, so colouring twice never compounds. `Companion.setShirt` keeps `this.outfit` current so a figure change refits the right colour.
- Checked once headless at seed 3: Amy on green and Jess on red, shirt hex matching `bodyColor`, no console errors. Not looked at by eye in the game, so how the yellow and silver-ish shades read on the model is untested.

## Jumpers from the barn (TODO 6)

- Pure logic in `src/npc/jumpers.js` (tests in `tests/jumpers.test.js`), driven from `Game.updateJumpers` the way tea is: it says who is to fetch or hang one, the game walks her to the rail with `sneakTo` and does the dressing when she gets there. "Cold" is the same test as warming her hands (`weather.warmth < 0.5` or rain over 0.25), but she has to have felt it for 25 s first, so a passing shower doesn't have her trotting to the barn, and 60 s of warm, dry weather before it comes off, so one doesn't flap on and off. A girl who's busy waits until she's stood about.
- She walks back to the rail to hang it up rather than dropping it, so the rail always shows what's really left. The rack (`src/world/jumperRail.js`, `layout.jumperRail`) is a freestanding clothes rack by the barn's south wall, clear of the bench, the ladder and the bay: a bar along the wall on two uprights, the jumpers on hangers edge-on to the wall. A girl stands at its east end. The debug panel's two jumper buttons (`Jumpers.toggle`) send a girl for one, or back to hang it up, whatever the weather; a jumper asked for that way stays on until asked to come off.
- The jumper is a `skinnedGarment` like the shorts: torso and arms of her own mesh, pushed 3 cm out (2.4 cm on the sleeves, so it's oversized), a hem 11 cm below the shorts' waistband. It stops at the wrist. The neck is round and low enough only to show the polo's collar and the top button: 6 cm below the neckline front and back, within 7.5 cm of the middle of her neck; shoulders and sleeves are covered right up (earlier goes at 17 cm and 10 cm were far too big). The body mesh is flat shaded, so its corner vertices each carry a different normal; at 3 cm the faces pulled apart into slits showing the polo through. `skinnedGarment` takes `{ smooth: true }`, which pushes each vertex along the average normal of everything at the same spot. Only the jumper uses it, so the shorts and collar haven't moved.
- The polo collar and placket stay on under it. `withJumper` adds or removes the colour on the outfit, so she goes back to exactly what she had, and `reshirt` and a figure change refit with the jumper still on.
- Colours are dull on purpose: grey `#7b7d80` and beige `#b7a98c`. Two lines each, `jumperOn` and `jumperOff`, in `jumperLines.js`: Jess says it smells of the barn, Amy says she looks like a sack.
- Checked once headless: both girls in jumpers close up, the rack from inside the barn, and in rain a girl walking to the rail and putting the grey one on with no console errors. Not checked: a jumper on a girl who's sitting on a quad or driving (the sleeves follow the same skeleton as the shirt, but I haven't looked at the arm poses), and the full hang-up trip back.
- Girls walk in a straight line, so going to the rack from outside went through the barn wall. `Companion.sneakTo` now takes `via` (waypoints, here two points leading in through the big doors) and `out` (a point to walk to afterwards, back outside). Checked once headless: Jess from the yard went in by the doorway, dressed, and came back out by it.

Closest quad-and-slot pairs are matched first so one already in place keeps its slot; a quad is tidy within 0.9 m and 0.25 rad of its slot. Amy's job is the untidy quad nearest her, putting off any whose slot has another quad in it.

- Debug panel: each girl has a live "tidying" line in the Girls folder (driving or nudging which quad, whether she goes on, or why she stopped). Shortcuts has Warm every engine, Start every quad (taps on, bowls topped up, straight to running and warm, no cranking) and Switch every quad off (ignition and fuel taps off).

## Nicking your quad (TODO 7)

- `src/npc/nicking.js` (pure, tested). The trigger is you getting off a quad with its engine still running: for `window` 30 s afterwards, from `minWait` 3 s, while you are 4 to 40 m from it and Jess is free and within 35 m, she rolls `chancePerSecond` 0.06 times her `nickChance` (Jess 1, Amy 0). One go per getting off, `cooldown` 360 s per girl, nothing before 120 s. It only counts a quad you have been riding, so a quad that was never yours, or one you switched off, is safe.
- She walks to it and mounts as for any drive, then `Companion.nickQuad` gives that drive a fixed length (`awayMin` 40 to `awayMax` 90 s, drawn from the game's seed). When it ends, `endDrive` doesn't dismount: it starts a second drive in HerDrive's `park` mode along the route back to the pose the quad had when you left it (the same point in line, then the spot), at `returnSpeed` 4 m/s, and she gets off there. She ends up within about 2 m of where it stood, not exactly on it.
- Muddier and thirstier: while she is away `quad.churn` is 0.35 (the mud effect doughnuts use, gentler: doughnuts are 0.7) and `extraBurn` 0.02 L/s comes off the tank on top of the normal burn, about 1.2 L for a minute. That is done in `game.updateNicking`, not in the drive.
- If you get on while she has it, the existing "taken" handling puts her on the back or off, and the job is dropped; so is it if she gives up or tips it. Only the clean finish of the away drive turns into the trip back.
- Lines (`nickLines.js`): Jess says `nickGo` setting off, `nickLaugh` every 14 s away, `nickBack` on returning; Amy says `nickWatch` 3 s after it starts if she is idle within 60 m, and Amy has `nickGo`, `nickLaugh` and `nickBack` lines only because every situation has to exist in her set; her `nickChance` is 0, so she never says them.
- Debug panel, Girls folder: "Jess nicks the nearest quad" (any free quad, running or not). Tuning panel has "Nicking your quad".
- Checked once headless with the away time cut to 12 s: she started the quad, drove it off (fuel 10.00 to 9.71 L, churn 0.35), drove back and got off 2.0 m from where it stood. Not checked: whether the trigger ever fires at a sensible rate in a real game, how the mud looks on the body afterwards, or how the lines sound in play.

## Hay ride (TODO 10)

Settled with Jamie before building: a kinematic trailer, not a physics body; hitched by `J` with the drawbar at its ring; the girls walk over and climb on by themselves once you're stopped; one slides off on a hard bend, runs after you and climbs back on; about four situations of lines, three each.

- **The trailer** (`vehicle/trailer.js`, pure, tested; `hayTrailer.js`, `trailerModel.js`). Not a Rapier body: the axle is dragged along behind the tractor's drawbar pin on a rigid 2.6 m drawbar (`followHitch`), which is all that's needed for the off-tracking swing on a corner and means it can't flip the tractor or snag on anything (and it doesn't collide with anything either: it goes through hedges and bales). Its body sits on springs on the axle (`springRate` 140, `springDamping` 7, driven by the axle's vertical acceleration from the terrain under both wheels), and pitches and rolls with the ground under the wheels and the pin. `jolt` is the spring's vertical speed, which is what throws the girls about. It stands in the yard at `LAYOUT.trailer` (8 m behind the tractor, pointing at it). 47 (the tipping trailer) can add carrying and handling on top of this.
- **Hitching.** `J` while riding the tractor: hitches when the pin is within `hitchRange` (1.1 m) of the ring, with a prompt when it is; unhitches when you're slower than 0.5 m/s; otherwise a toast. The trailer snaps onto the line behind the pin in a frame, so up to 1.1 m of lurch on hitching.
- **Who's aboard** (`npc/hayRide.js`, pure, tested). Active when the trailer is hitched and you're riding the tractor. A girl who is stood about with nothing else on, within 40 m, sets out 1 to 4 s after the tractor has stopped (under 0.6 m/s) and takes the free side nearer her; two girls, two seats, so no more than two. She walks to beside the wheel, climbs up (`companionHayRide.js`, new states `boarding`, `onTrailer`, `sliding`; the other behaviours only start from `idle` so they leave her alone) and sits on the top bale at the edge of the well, facing forward. She waits at the trailer if it's still moving. When you get off or unhitch each one climbs down after up to 0.9 s.
- **Bumps.** Over `bumpJolt` (0.5 m/s of bounce) one of them shrieks (Jess 65% of the time), and both throw their arms up for 0.7 s. At most once every 14 s. On flat grass the jolt stays near zero, so bumps only come with rough ground.
- **Lying back.** After `lieAfter` (7 s) of going at over 1.2 m/s, nearly straight (under 0.1 rad/s) and smooth (jolt under 0.25), there's a 50% chance one lies back (the pivot at her hips leans back 1.15 rad, hands behind her head), then another try after the same time. A bump over 0.6 or a sideways push over 1.2 m/s squared sits her up again.
- **Falling off.** Each girl has a grip from 1 to 0. The sideways push toward her edge is speed times the trailer's turn rate (in a left turn everyone is thrown to the right, so the girl on the right is on the outside), plus a share of the jolt. Over her limit (`holdPush` 2.4 m/s squared, 1.5 lying back, times her own nerve of 0.85 to 1.3) the grip drains at 0.45 a second per m/s squared over, and comes back at 0.5 a second when calm. At zero, and not in the first 12 s aboard, and not within 90 s of the last fall, there's a 60% chance she goes; otherwise she hangs on with 0.4 grip left. For scale, a left bend at 6 m/s with the trailer turning at 0.5 rad/s is 3 m/s squared, a gentle one at 3 m/s and 0.3 rad/s is 0.9, which never drops anyone. She slides off feet first, lands 3.2 m from the middle on her side, lies in the mud for about 1.5 s, gets up, and runs after the trailer at 1.7 times her walking pace and climbs on when it is slower than 1.2 m/s. The other says something 1.2 s after the landing. There's no mud on her afterwards.
- **Lines** (`hayRideLines.js`): `hayBoard`, `hayBump`, `hayLie`, `hayFall` (the one who fell, on landing) and `hayFallOther`, three lines each for each girl, recorded. Tunables are under Hay ride, Hay ride seat and Trailer in the tuning panel.
- **Checked:** unit tests (the hitch following, off-tracking in a turn, springs and bumps, the sign of the sideways push, boarding, leaving, bumps, lying back, falling in each direction with the grace period, cooldown and the lying case); the game loads with no page errors; headless, driving the tractor by teleporting it along a heading: the prompt and hitching, both girls walking over and climbing on, the trailer following in a straight line and swinging round a left turn at 0.9 rad/s, a forced lie back and a forced slide off (fall, lie, rise, walk back and board in about 3 s), screenshots of the seating and the fall. **Not checked:** driving it with real controls, a real fall from a real bend (my turn at 3 m/s didn't push hard enough to drop anyone, which suggests it wants a fast third-gear bend), how the lying pose and the arms behind her head look from close up, how the landing looks on a slope, the girls' lines by ear, or the trailer in a bog. Left alone: the girls on the mudguard (11), a chain to the quads (12), carrying things and tipping (47).

Each stop is rolled once (`boardChance` 0.55) so she doesn't pester you; if it comes up, one free girl within 30 m (Jess 60% when both are free) sets out after 1.5 to 5 s. `maxAboard` is 1 for these, so it's never both unprompted. It doesn't check her mood: a neglected girl doesn't refuse.

## Jess and the tractor (TODO 14)

Settled with Jamie before building: Jess asks herself (she just takes it) and it's also on the wheel; a short wander round the field, then parked; the kangaroo and stalls are real physics, "can't see over the bonnet" is a lean and a line; Amy refuses until a game-time day and then drives well; both on the wheel.

- **It reuses her quad driving.** `requestDrive(tractor quad)` walks her to it and `HerDrive` drives it: the tractor is a Quad with a manual drivetrain, which already had a girl's clutch (`AutoShifter`), so nothing in the physics is new. The tractor isn't in `girlUnits`, so the game calls it directly (`startTractorGo`), keeps her own quad in `homeQuad` and gives it back when she's idle again so everything else that looks at her quad finds one of the girls'.
- **Starting** (`npc/tractorStart.js`, pure, tested). `HerDrive.beginStart` uses `TractorStartAttempt` in place of `StartingAttempt` for the tractor: key on after 0.8 s, wait for the glow lamp, crank in bursts of 2.5 to 4.5 s, give up after five. On her first go she cranks before the lamp half the time (`rushChance`, which only works on a warm engine); after one failure she always waits. No tap or choke.
- **Jess's clutch** (`NoviceShifter` in `vehicle/autoShifter.js`, tested). The same lever and pedal as the good driver's, but she never goes above second, sets off in second 35% of the time (and stays there rather than dropping back to first), 60% of set-offs kangaroo (the clutch comes up in three fits of 0.24 s) and half of the second-gear set-offs she lifts off the throttle as the clutch bites (`throttleScale` 0 for 0.9 s), which stalls it through the tractor's own launch load; first never stalls it. `Quad.shift` returns the throttle so it can apply that. Amy gets the ordinary `AutoShifter`: no hops, no stalls. `setDriver(null)` puts an `AutoShifter` back on the tractor.
- **Driving.** Jess 1.4 to 3.4 m/s, Amy 2.5 to 5.5, neither scared off by speed, 40 to 70 s, at most 0.7 throttle; Jess gives up after six stalls. Leaving it running when she gets off follows the quads' `driveKeyOffChance` (the stop knob is the tractor's way of switching off).
- **When** (`npc/tractorGo.js`, pure, tested). Jess: not before 150 s, not within 300 s of anyone's last go, 3% a second once she can, only when you're not on it and nobody's on or going to it, nothing's hitched and she's free. Amy's day: from 1,200 s, once Jess has had a go, 90 s after the last one; she says "Fine. Move over", drives it, and `amyCan` is true from then on. Asked before that, Amy refuses with `tractorRefuse`. A refused ask doesn't count against you. All in the tuning panel under Tractor go.
- **Lines** (`tractorGoLines.js`): `tractorGoAsk`, `tractorGoSetOff`, `tractorGoLurch`, `tractorGoStall`, `tractorGoDone`, `tractorGoWatch` for both, plus Amy's `tractorRefuse` and `tractorDay`. The other girl comments on a hop (1.4 s later) and four seconds after a set-off. Re-recorded.
- **Checked:** unit tests for the start attempt, the novice shifter and the when-logic; headless, Jess sent for a go on the warm tractor (walked over, mounted, key on, started, set off in second with a hop, drove loops at 1.5 to 2.2 m/s for about a minute and a half, stopped, got off, left it running) with no page errors. **Not checked:** a stall actually happening (none did in my one run), Amy's day, a cold start with the glow wait and an early crank, how the lean looks, how the kangaroo looks and feels (the clutch fits are the only mechanism, no impulse), her mounting and dismounting the tractor from close up (the climb is the quads' and the seat is high), the wheel slices in play, her avoiding the hedge or the trailer when she can't see, the lines by ear.

### Her hands on the wheel

The tractor's steering wheel has a `gripLeft` and `gripRight` on its rim at nine and three o'clock, which turn with the wheel, so the quads' hand pose (`poseDriving`) puts both of a girl's hands on it while she drives, for Amy as well as Jess. Looked at once headless from the side and the front: both hands on the rim. Not checked: how it looks at full lock, or her reach on the short seat position.


## Showing off (TODO 16)

- `src/npc/showOff.js` (pure, tested in `tests/showOff.test.js`): `ShowOffRun` is a small state machine (setup, run, look) that `HerDrive.driveShowOff` runs as a drive mode, like doughnuts and the jump. Setup finds a clear point 32 m from you (the way she is already facing you if it can, otherwise trying either side and behind with `isClear`) and gets there at her usual pedal feel; the run goes at a mark 4 m to one side of you at 9 m/s. A wheelie starts 17 m from the mark: full throttle and the nose up for 1.8 s. A skid works out where to stand on the brakes (speed squared over twice 6 m/s squared, plus 1.2 m) and locks them from there. Then she stops and looks for 3 s.
- Wheelies are visual only. `quad.wheelie` (radians, 0.5) tips the body and lifts the front wheels about the rear axle, eased in and out; the physics doesn't know. A real wheelie would need a pitching torque model and a way to catch her before she flips, and it isn't worth it for something that lasts two seconds. A wheelie is only picked on a two-stroke (70%); any other quad skids.
- Whether you saw: `youSaw` wants her inside a cone of about 57 degrees either side of where your eyes point (the camera) and within 80 m, for a total of 0.35 s from the start of the trick to the end of the look. Seen, she's pleased and stops. Unseen, she goes round to the other side and does it again, three goes at most, then says you never look. The cone is the camera's, so it works riding or on foot.
- Deciding: `boredShowOffChance` (Jess 0.12, Amy 0.04), only if her drive confidence is at least 0.5, you are within 70 m, and there is a free non-tractor quad. She can say no room and give up if no clear run exists (the yard is cramped, so she mostly does it in the field). If something shows up ahead for 1.5 s she brakes and gives up.
- Lines in `showOffLines.js` (deciding, the trick, seen, again, missed), recorded into both banks.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and headless once: Jess on the yellow two-stroke, placed in the open field 27 m back from you. The wheelie lifts to 0.5 rad as she passes, she stops beside you, and when you are facing away she goes again (twice, then the third time stops after the look); the skid stops her about 4 m off with you facing her, and she calls it seen and stops. **Not checked:** how the wheelie looks (the screenshot caught the game's start overlay), whether the lifted nose clips her figure, whether the skid shows any slide, Amy's version, and the lines in play. She stalled on the run-up in the cramped yard in an early run (a manual two-stroke braked to a stop by the obstacle check), which is why obstacles give up the go rather than just braking.

## Left running (TODO 15)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking. Nothing new makes a girl wander off: after she starts a quad (head start, or you ask her to) she already goes idle with it running, and the heat and fuel work already finish the job. What was missing was anyone noticing, and being right.

- `src/npc/leftRunning.js` (pure, tests in `tests/leftRunning.test.js`; tunables under "Left running"). The game says each frame, for each quad, whether it's running, whether anyone is on it or starting it, and who is at the controls (you, a girl driving, or a girl having a go at starting it). The last of those to have it is the one to blame, so a girl who starts it for you and goes off is the culprit, and so are you if you step off without switching it off. A passenger alone doesn't change the blame. After `askAfter` 75 s running unattended it counts as left, once per stretch, in a tally per person.

Same shape as the wear blame: each girl has her own table, and the situation named for her own name is her owning up. Amy minds the petrol; Jess is unbothered and tells on Amy with relish.

- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how long 75 s feels, how often the lines fire, or how they sound.

## Liking them running, the seat's buzz and side-saddle (2026-10-07)

Not a TODO item: Jamie asked for it alongside item 15. Asked first; the answers were the recommended ones.

- `src/npc/contentment.js` (pure, tests in `tests/contentment.test.js`), one per girl on `companion.content`. Tunables are in her companion params, so they're in each girl's panel and can differ per character.
- Stalls: a quad stalling within her voice range adds `sadOnStall` 0.4 (0.8 if it's under her), fading at `sadFade` 0.012 a second, so about a minute and a half. She says `stallSad` (unless the existing stalled-while-riding lines already did) and, when it catches again with her still down, `stallRelief`. A stall while she was driving knocks her driving confidence by `stallConfidenceKnock` 0.04.
- Liking them running: her mood (`joy`) follows the share of the quads running. With 70% or more going and her stood or resting within 8 m of a running one, she says `likeAllRunning`; with half or fewer, `wishAllRunning`. The bored 'start' weight is multiplied by 1 plus `startUrge` 1.5 times the share not running, so with all of them off she starts one 2.5 times as often as when all are on. The chance she switches off an idling quad she's sat on is cut by up to `keyOffCut` 0.8 of her joy. She still never starts one you've asked her to leave.
- The buzz: the body-shake level from `unit.vibration.body` (the one the shaking comments already use) counts once it's over `buzzFrom` 0.5, capped at 2, as shake-seconds per quad. After `buzzSaysAfter` 25 of them on one quad she says `buzzLove` (once per sitting). Her favourite quad moves to the one with the most, once it leads her current one by `buzzSwitchLead` 40, and only to one she has favourite lines for. A warm idle is about 0.6 on that measure, so a few minutes on any idling quad counts; the rough two-strokes count for more.
- Side-saddle (`src/npc/sideSaddle.js`, tests in `tests/sideSaddle.test.js`): each time she's sent to the back she picks it with `sideSaddleChance` (Amy 0.4, Jess 0.1, and a side at random). She turns her hips `sideSaddleTurn` 1.0 rad towards the side her legs hang, the back twists 80% of the way round again to face forward, and her legs come together rather than astride. The turn is on the root, so the seat offset is rotated with her to keep her hips on the seat.
- Slipping: only above 3 m/s. A sideways push over `slipLateral` 5 m/s squared (thrown the way her legs hang; half as much counts the other way), or a bump or landing over `slipBump` 25, builds slip at `slipRate` per second per unit over, and she recovers at 0.5 a second when it eases. Slip 0.5 is a yelp (`sideSaddleWobble`), 1 is off (`sideSaddleSlip`): she goes into the dismount and lands on the ground beside where the quad was, which carries on without her. With you riding in front her arms are round you and the limits are 1.6 times higher, so she holds through most of what would throw her alone. Nothing falls off while astride.
- Left out: a tumble animation (she just slides into the usual dismount), her hands taking a different hold side-saddle (the arms still reach the same targets, only the body is turned), and any effect on you riding off without her afterwards beyond the lines she already has.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game, so the pose is the biggest unknown: whether the turned hips sit right on every quad's seat and look sensible, whether the arms reach you from the twisted body, whether the slip thresholds are about right for real riding, and the girls' new lines.


## A burnt leg on the exhaust (TODO 76)

Nothing needed asking; I took the item as written.

The quads' silencers run down the right (`exhaustSideOf`: -1, or a spec's `exhaustSide`); the tractor, UTV, Land Rovers and cars have none to catch a leg on (0). Pipe heat is the larger of the engine's `heat` and `soak`, so a quad that has just worked or sat in the sun is hot and one left an hour is not.

She normally gets off on the left; `exhaustSideChance` (Jess 0.3, Amy 0) has her swing off the right when the left is free. If her friend is on the left, either girl goes right, as before, but only Jess burns: `burnChance` is 0.8 for Jess and 0 for Amy, who holds her leg clear. Under `burnFrom` 0.35 of heat nothing happens; above it the chance rises with the heat.
- When it burns, as the leg goes over (past half way through the move) she yelps (`burntLeg`, a self event, both girls have lines though Amy's only come from the debug button) and on landing she flinches away through the ordinary reaction. No injury and no lasting effect, so nothing is kept in the save. She does it again another day because each getting off is a fresh roll; she learns nothing.
- Debug: "Burns her leg on the exhaust" in each girl's Girls folder (and `girl <name> burn-leg`) gets her off over the exhaust side, hot or not, if she is sat on a quad. The three tunables are in her companion params.

## Amy's logbook (TODO 20)

The item settled the idea, so I chose the details without asking.

- Pure logic in `src/workshop/logbook.js` (tests in `tests/logbook.test.js`): `Logbook.write` turns "what happened" into a line like "Blue, belt slipping." and drops repeats of the same thing about the same quad inside `repeatSeconds` (120), so a quad she walks round every ride doesn't fill the page. The page keeps the last `maxEntries` (40). Time is seconds since the game began, shown as m:ss. Tunables are under "Logbook".
- What goes in: her walk-round findings and oil top-ups (she did those herself, so always written), and the plug you pulled and the bench jobs you finish (written only if she is within `writeRange`, 15 m, which is her voice range for the bench lines, so she has to have seen it). Jobs and plugs are marked "(you)"; a botched job says "not well". The plug is written as what it really is (`readPlug`), not what a girl guessed, which is the point of the book being more honest than Jess.
- The prop and the page are in `src/workshop/notebook.js`: a red notebook on the bench, 0.9 m along from the toolbox, read with `E`. The page is a card low on the screen like the bench panel, so the barn stays in view, and shows the newest entries at the bottom.
- Left out: Jess's mistakes (she writes nothing), quirks and history (Amy doesn't know them for certain, so a line would be a lie or a spoiler), faults she hears or smells away from the bench, and the notebook surviving a reload. A girl can't pick it up or tease you about it yet. Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how the notebook looks on the bench, whether the card reads well, or that the interact box beats the bench's own.


Starting entries (added after Jamie asked for them): `startingCandidates` and `startingEntries` in `logbook.js` pick `startMin` to `startMax` (2 to 5) from what is true of each vehicle at the start: history faults, tinkering, quirks and the last rider. Each is kept with `startKnows` (0.85), so she misses some and never writes what isn't true, and at most one note of who had a quad out is kept because nearly every quad has one. They come from the seed (`seed ^ 0x109b`), show as "Yesterday", and don't count towards the two-minute repeat rule, so a walk-round finding of the same fault is still written. Plug readings aren't pre-written, since the plug is read at the bench. Decided with Jamie: quirks are included, and Jess writes none. Checked: unit tests only; not how it reads in the running game.

## Amy never turns it off (2026-10-07)

## The collar lowered, and the jumpers remodelled (2026-10-07)

Jamie: the girls' collars sat too high, and should line up with the base of the neck; the jumpers looked strange, worn and on the rack.

- **The collar.** The model's shirt colour runs up her neck like a roll neck, to 1.25 m with her neck bone at 1.20 and her jaw at about 1.28, and the collar was fitted to the top of it, so it sat nearly under her chin. The body is low-poly: the triangles that carry the shirt up her neck run from the tops of her shoulders. `lowerNeckline` gives the skin's palette cell to any shirt triangle at the front or sides that touches her neck (a corner within 7.5 cm of its middle), stays within 10.5 cm and reaches more than 3 cm above the neck bone. Those further out at the back are left alone: they reach past the collar's flap and showed as skin patches on her shoulders. The collar now follows the shirt's top all the way round (the highest shirt point in each slice of angle, smoothed), so it sits at the base of her neck at the front and a little higher at the back, as a polo collar does. Once per body geometry, which both girls share.
- **The worn jumper.** It was her body mesh pushed out 3 cm with flat shading, so it came out angular, with sharp facets over her bust. `skinnedGarment` now takes `soft` (the corners at each spot welded into one vertex, smooth normals: one soft shell), `shape` (moves each vertex in root space) and `shade` (vertex colours). The jumper is soft, 2.6 cm off her body (sleeves 2.1), and below her bust the front falls straight instead of following her under it, easing back towards a snug ribbed hem (up to 5 cm forward, measured from her chest's furthest-forward point across a 12 cm window so it bridges between her breasts). Cuffs, hem and neck are ribbed bands 24% darker. The crew neck still shows the polo's collar and top button.
- **On the rack.** The hanging jumpers were boxes. Each is now one smooth knitted shape built from rings (`hangingJumperRings` in `src/world/jumperRail.js`, pure and tested): a flattened body that is fuller through the middle and gathered at a ribbed hem, with soft folds that deepen lower down; shoulders sloping from a ribbed round neck that dips at the front; sleeves leaving the shoulder seams and hanging beside the body with a slight bend into ribbed cuffs. A wire hook over the bar and wooden hanger arms that follow the shoulders inside it. The hem and cuffs are closed with a dark inside so they don't look hollow from below.
- Checked headless (overlay hidden, camera placed by script): both girls' necks from the front, side, back and above; both in jumpers from the front, back and close up; Amy in a jumper sitting on the red; the rack from four angles; no page or console errors. Not checked: Jess sitting in a jumper, a girl in a jumper riding on the back or front rack, and the shirt colours other than the ones her favourite quad gave her this seed.

## Shouting a fault (2026-10-07)

Item 7. The item settled the idea; I chose the rest without asking: it is signs a rider can't see from the saddle, shouted only while you ride, and no new faults.

- `src/npc/shoutFault.js` (`FaultShout`, tests in `tests/shoutFault.test.js`, tunables under "Shouting a fault"). A girl who is off your quad and free to look (following, stood about, or on her own quad) shouts when your running quad is moving over `minSpeed` 2.5 m/s, she is within `seeRange` 28 m, and she is behind you or alongside (no more than `aheadSlack` 1.5 m in front of your middle: ahead of you she sees your face, not your back). She needs it in view for `amySeeFor` 1.2 s or `jessSeeFor` 2.6 s, looking away resets that, she shouts at most once every `gap` 12 s, and the same thing at most once every `sameGap` 70 s. Worst first when several show: steam, hanging, smoke, wobble, tyre.
- What counts as seen: steam off the radiator (`steam` 0.25), blue smoke from too much oil (`oilRich` 0.5) or black from diesel (`dieselSoak` 0.3), a sagging tyre (puncture level 0.35, before you feel it through the bars), a flat one wobbling on the rim (0.7), and a blown silencer "hanging off" (`silencerBlown` 0.5). A two-stroke's ordinary smoke is not a fault. "Wobble" is the flat tyre at its worst; I added no wobble of its own.
- The lines (`shoutFaultLines.js`) are all shouted, so they carry further, and are separate from the saddle remarks in `watchFaults`, which stay for when she is on or by the quad.
- Checked: unit tests. Not checked: how it reads in the running game, whether 28 m is the right distance, or how often it fires against the saddle remarks. The numbers are picked, not tuned. The lines still need recording.

## The collar, cut on a clean neckline; a rounder bust (2026-10-07)

Jamie: the collar's top should sit where the neck meets the shoulders, where the placket starts, and it had become far too wide; then, skin showed on her shoulders between the shirt and the collar; and make the busts a little rounder.

- **Why recolouring triangles wasn't enough.** The body is so coarse that the triangles carrying the shirt up her neck run from her shoulders to near her jaw, so whole triangles give a ragged, lopsided neckline: after the last change the shirt still reached 1.25 m on her left and stopped out on her chest at the front.
- **The neckline cut.** `cutNeckline` widens the girls' texture to twice its width, with a strip in the new half that is shirt colour in its lower half and skin in its upper half (`withNecklineStrip`; the palette's own colours, so recolouring the shirt recolours it too). Every other UV is squeezed into the left half, and each shirt triangle round her neck is mapped across the strip by its height above the neckline at its angle, so the shirt changes to skin exactly on the line, wherever it crosses a triangle. The line (`necklineAt`) is 3.6 cm above the neck bone round the sides and back, dipping 4 cm at the front where the placket starts. Height above the line counts distance out from her neck five times over beyond 6.5 cm, so the line stays on her neck and the tops of her shoulders stay shirt; at twice that, a wedge of skin showed on each shoulder.
- **The collar.** A short stand round her neck from just under the neckline, a crease (its top edge) 1.1 cm above it, and a flap lying back on the shirt no more than 3.6 cm out (`COLLAR_REACH`), lifted on to whatever is under it rather than following her shoulders outwards. Its two ends meet over the top of the placket (`COLLAR_GAP` 0.12 rad), and the placket starts just under them. The old thickened band of shirt under the collar is gone.
- **Two gotchas.** Three.js's `BufferGeometry.clone` shares `userData` with the original, so a flag set there on Amy's geometry was also set on Jess's and her neckline was never cut; geometries cut are kept in a WeakSet instead. (`figure.js` keeps `bindPositions` in the same shared object; harmless, since both girls start from the same positions.) And her hair can be its own mesh on the same material, so every mesh on the texture is found before any is changed, or the hair stays unsqueezed and samples the strip (Jess's hair came out the colour of her shirt).
- **Rounder bust.** The bust's forward push used the same smooth taper as its swell, which on this mesh came out conical. It now follows a dome, `(1 - e) ** 0.55` faded in at the edge, so it's full across the middle and falls away at the edge, and the region reaches less far below the apex (0.22 of a hips-to-neck length, was 0.27), so it's less of a teardrop. In profile she's fuller above the apex and rounder underneath.
- Checked headless: both girls close up front, three-quarter and back, from your eye height either side, and in profile; the jumper over the new collar. Not checked: the collar with other shirt colours, and how the strip looks from far off, where the texture's mipmaps blend its two halves.

## No collar; rounder still (2026-10-07)

After several goes the collar still wasn't right (asked for: buttoned up, closed at the front), so Jamie had it removed: the polo has no collar, and the placket starts at the top of the shirt.

- **What stays.** The neckline cut (`cutNeckline` and its texture strip), so the shirt stops on a clean line round the base of her neck instead of running up it like a roll neck. With nothing to cover it, the line dips only 1.5 cm at the front (`NECK_DIP`, was 4): deeper, it cut across a ledge of the model's old roll neck at her throat and showed as a slash of skin. The placket starts on the line at the front, with its top button just under it (`shirtFrontTop`, buttons at 10%, 45% and 80% of its length).
- **What went.** The folded collar (`addCollar`, `collarSection` and its test), which on this model kept either climbing her neck, spreading over her shoulders, leaving skin showing between its halves, or fitting to the inner of the two shells the model has at her throat.
- **Bust, rounder again.** The forward push's dome is `(1 - e) ** 0.35` (was 0.55) and the region reaches 0.2 below the apex (was 0.22). Doming the swell as well made the shape steeper than `tests/figure.test.js` allows (4.2 times a small move, against 4), so only the forward push is domed.
- Checked headless: both girls close up front, three-quarter and back, from your eye height, and in jumpers.
- Follow-up: the placket still started above where the shirt meets her skin, running up over the skin at the base of her neck. I had placed it at `necklineAt`'s front height, but the cut's boundary sits lower there (it is bent by distance out from her neck), and I judged it from a scaled-down contact sheet. `shirtFrontTop` now measures it: down the middle of her front, the first point whose texture colour is the shirt's. Checked at full resolution, front and three-quarter, on both girls.
- Jumper neck, smoother: the rule for where the jumper stops round her neck stepped from scooped to over-the-shoulders 8 cm out, which made a square notch, and its edge followed whole coarse triangles, which saw-toothed. The line is now a smooth curve (lowest at the front, a little low at the back), and `skinnedGarment`'s `smoothEdge` moves each point on the neck opening's edge towards the middle of its two neighbours along it, six times. Checked at full resolution on both girls.

## Girls staying full size

- Report: after getting in the Land Rover the girls sometimes ended up too big, and more so each time.
- Cause (found by reading, not reproduced): climbing off uses `Object3D.attach`, which keeps the world transform, so any stretch in the vehicle's chain is baked into the girl's own scale. The vehicle's rotation comes straight from Rapier as a quaternion that is never renormalised, and a slightly long one stretches its matrix; the girl then carries that stretch and takes it into the next vehicle too, so it multiplies.
- Fix: `attachKeepingSize` (`src/npc/attachKeepingSize.js`, tested) attaches and puts her scale back to one, used for every attach of a girl (getting off, sliding off a trailer, shuffling on a quad). The chassis quaternion is also normalised when recorded (`recordTransform`). I didn't confirm the drift in the running game, so if it comes back the cause is somewhere else.

## Getting on and off, and standing about (TODO 7)

Jamie agreed: poses written in code rather than CC0 clips (Quaternius's library uses a different skeleton, so retargeting would be fiddly); three moves used everywhere; longer moves are fine; a few ways of standing about for each girl.

- **Poses in code** (`src/npc/poseMoves.js`, pure, tested): a pose is bone turns about her own axes plus hands placed by the arm IK that already held the bars (`armIK.js`), optionally with the hand turned (folded arms need the fingers tucked round the other arm, or they stick out past the elbows). A child bone turns about its parent's turned axes, so "bend the knee" means the same however the hip is turned. `CharacterRig.applyPose` layers it on after the clips and before the older offsets and posers, so fidgets, the pillion hold and the grips still sit on top.
- **Moves**: leg over (any quad, the ATC, the mini moto, the front seat), hop on (side-saddle and the front rack: back to the seat, hands behind, a hop, a swivel), step in (the UTV's and Land Rover's bench: foot on the sill, ducking, swung round), climb up (the bale trailer, the tractor's arches and seat, the UTV's bed). Each is keys over 0 to 1 giving the hips' path (`over`, `along`, `lift`), the blend from standing to sitting (`sit`), the turn, and a pose. Authored from the seat's left and mirrored for the right; getting off plays the same keys backwards.
- **The path is the hips', not the root's** (`src/npc/companionMoves.js`): the root goes wherever puts her hips on the path, given how far the Idle and SitIdle clips are blended (`CharacterRig.mix`). That keeps her bottom on the seat at the end however the blend goes, and makes the move land exactly on the seated position, so there's no snap into the seat.
- **Footboard first**: `along` (the fore-and-aft share) lags `over` on a quad, so she stays over the footboard while her leg goes over and only then shifts back onto the pillion. Getting on and stepping off are now from the middle of the footboard (z 0, 0.1 for the front seat) instead of beside the back wheel (z -0.5), where her foot went through the arch.
- **Called off part-way** (asked to get down while still climbing on): getting off runs back from where she'd got to (`from`), so there's no jump.
- Side-saddle, her legs now go over the side she got on from, rather than a side picked at random.
- Timings: 1.8 s on and 1.4 s off a quad (were 1.2 and 1.0), 1.8 s climbing (0.9), 2 s stepping into a bench. Lifts: 0.3 m onto a footrest, 0.32 m onto a sill, the seat's own (0.45 to 0.7 m) for climbing. All in the "Getting on and off" and "Hay ride seat" tuning folders.
- **Sat**: astride a quad the thighs drop 0.3 rad and the knees bend a further 0.25, so the feet sit back on the footrests; a touch more relaxed on a bench; knees together side-saddle.
- **Standing about** (`StandingIdles`): a gap of 2 to 7 s, eased in over 0.7 s, held 4 to 10 s (a stretch 2.2 s), eased out; never the same one twice running; dropped quickly when she does anything else. Amy: arms folded, hands clasped, weight on one foot. Jess: hands on hips, rocking on her heels, scuffing a toe, a stretch, a hand in her back pocket. Lists in `characters.js`. The older standing fidgets (a lean, a look round) still happen on top.
- Checked: unit tests (the moves' data, mirroring, sampling, the idle chooser, and the runner landing on the seat, at the step-off spot and with no jump when called off), and headless screenshots with the game stepped by hand: the leg over on and off from three sides, side-saddle, Jess onto the front, the UTV and Land Rover benches, the UTV's bed on and off, and every standing idle close up. Not checked: the bale trailer, the tractor's arches and seat, the rack, the mini moto and ATC, and a slide off side-saddle at speed. Bodies still pass through the Land Rover's doors (they don't open).

## Dozing in the sun (TODO 80)

Decided with Jamie: a sixth need, sleepiness, rather than a one-off chance; both girls doze, Jess sooner; a bite-sized snore; woken gently, rudely or by themselves; a bucket-list photo.

- **Sleepiness** (`sleep` in `needs.js`, labelled "sleepiness", 0 met to 1 pressing). It rises all the time (`needSleepRise`, half as fast again while she is on the go) and only dozing lowers it (`needSleepFall`). Jess rises at 0.0016 a second against Amy's 0.0009, so Jess is pressing in about 10 minutes of play and Amy in about 18. The rest need is kept: sitting down rests her, only a doze cures her sleepiness.
- **What it does to her.** `sleepySlowThink` (2): her thinking gap is multiplied by 1 plus that times her sleepiness, so a worn-out girl takes up to three times as long between thoughts. `sleepyLazy` (0.6): every offer except a doze or an urgent one (shelter) is weighted by 1 minus that times her sleepiness, so she does less. Both are in `updateMinds`.
- **The doze is an offer** (`doze` in `OFFER_KINDS`: meets sleep fully and rest by half, so it only clears `chooseMin` when she is properly sleepy). `places.js` makes it to a girl stood about when it is fine and dry, not dark and the sun is at least `dozeSunAbove` (0.45; `Game.sunWarmth`), to a free straw bale only (not the fence, the loft or the barn). Amy's `dozeWeight` is 0.8, Jess's 1.2.
- **The doze itself** is the resting state with `doze: true` (no new state, so availability treats her as sat resting and nothing else starts on her). `src/npc/dozing.js` (pure, tested) has the tuning (in the "Places" tune group), who wakes to what (`WAKERS`: a rev within 14 m, a horn 40, a bang or backfire 60, a tip-over 40, a close crack of thunder and so on; she is a little lighter while still nodding, 1.5 times the range) and the head's shape (`dozePose`). She nods for `dozeDropSeconds` (7) then hangs her head (new `headDown` rig offset), stops looking about and stops fidgeting. She dozes for 90 to 240 seconds at most and wakes herself earlier when her sleepiness is down to 0.08 or the sun goes in.
- **Waking.** By herself: a line (Amy was only resting her eyes), a few seconds more sat, then she gets up as from any rest. Gently (you press `E` and she is dozing): her own line instead of the usual "hang on", then the talk menu. Rudely (`wakeToNotice`, from any notice in `WAKERS`): a flinch, a cross line, she stays sat a few seconds. Amy takes that badly (`dozeMinds`: 3 favour and a 30 second sulk, both in the Places tuning), Jess laughs.
- **Snoring** (`src/audio/snoreAudio.js`): a procedural breath every 3.6 seconds, noise in then a low flutter out, only audible within `dozeSnoreRange` (6 m). Nothing recorded.
- **Photos.** A girl asleep does not pose for the camera, and a photo with one asleep in it counts for the bucket-list item "Caught napping" (`photoAsleep`).
- **Debug.** Her sleepiness is settable among her needs; the Girls tab has buttons to send her to doze, wake her gently and wake her with a bang (`girl <name> doze`, `wake`, `wake-rudely`). The tuning is under "Places" and in the girls' needs group.
- **Not done:** she does not lie down (it is the existing sitting pose with her head dropped); she only dozes outside, on the bales; no drool, no "Z"s.
- Checked: unit tests only (`tests/dozing.test.js`); the head droop and the snore were not looked at or listened to.

Amy: arms folded, hands on knees, ankles crossed, leant back on her hands, elbow out. Jess: a jiggling knee, hands behind her head, legs swinging out of step, slouched, leant back, elbow out.

Elbow out never mirrors, so it's always on the door side.

- Checked: unit tests, and headless shots of each in the UTV's window seat and on its tailgate, and the elbow on the Land Rover's door. Hands behind the head first sat on top of it, and the elbow first put her hand by her face; both lowered. Not checked: the bales, the tub, the arches and the rack.

Now `Companion.startCarry` (`src/npc/companionCarrying.js`) takes an item's model, the right arm reaches for it with the existing two-bone IK, and each frame the model is placed so its grip sits at her wrist and hangs upright, turned with her. `src/npc/carrying.js` (pure, tested) holds the stages (raise 0.7 s, hold, lower 0.7 s), the weight (a can by how full, the toolbox 0.7, the mug 0.05), the pace (can 1 down to 0.78 full, toolbox 0.88, mug 0.95) and the lean (0.09 rad at full weight, away from the hand). All of it is in a shared `carryParams` with ranges.
- Prank: she stops an arm's length from the item (not 1.3 m), picks it up, carries it to the hiding spot and sets it down there, and the prank only counts as done once it's down (`sneakGiveUp` raised from 90 to 150 s to allow the walk). The ten-minute restore does the same in reverse if she's free, otherwise the item goes back at once as before. While she has it, a flag stops you picking it up and the prank code counts it as held.
- Tea: she brings a new mug from the house in her hand, lowers it onto the rack, and on the way back lifts it off, has a sip (2.2 s, hand to her mouth) and puts it down, where it is removed.
- Called away mid-carry (a request, the bell, getting on a quad) she sets it down at her side; carrying girls don't count as free for the tea and pranks.

Not checked: the tea in the running game, the pickup bend by eye (the first frame of the lift was behind a prompt), whether the lean reads the right way, a full prank end to end, or being interrupted mid-carry. The hand sits at a fixed spot, so the fingers don't wrap round the handle.

## The girls no longer shove the quads (TODO 5)

- Cause: a girl is a kinematic body set to her position each frame, so when she walks, stands or steps up against a quad she overlaps it and Rapier pushes the quad out of the way (kinematic bodies are immovable). The player doesn't, because the character controller stops short of things.
- Fix: her collider has solver groups `0xffff0000` (the same as the player's while stumbling): it makes no contact forces, so it can't move anything. The player's controller still treats her as solid, and her ray probes still see the quads, so her routing is unchanged. The cost is that a quad driven into her passes through rather than stopping.
- `tests/companionNoShove.test.js` shows in Rapier itself that a hand-moved kinematic capsule shoves a dynamic box by default and doesn't with these groups.
- Not done: no extra avoidance logic. The existing probes already stop her walking into quads; the shoving was the contact response when she ended up touching one. **Not checked:** in the running game.

## The girls drive the UTV and the Land Rover (TODO 10)

Settled with Jamie before building: one go for all three vehicles (Jess picks a free one, Amy's single day unlocks all three); the Land Rover's starting handle on a flat battery, Jess kicked back, Amy never; crash gears: Jess grinds and Amy double-declutches; the other girl may join on the bench; UTV 2 to 6 m/s, Land Rover 2 to 8, 40 to 70 s a go, Jess never above second. Added mid-build: either girl, Amy included, will start any of the three when asked, even before she will drive.

- **The scheduler stays `TractorGo`** (`npc/tractorGo.js`) and now gets the list of free vehicles; the event says which (`vehicle`). `game.goVehicle`, `goKindOf`, `goFree`, `startGo`, `askDriveVehicle`, `canAskStart` are the game's side; `startGo` keeps her own quad in `homeQuad` as for the tractor. The tractor-only names (`updateTractorGo`, `tractorSeen`) were kept to keep the diff small.
- **Driver's door.** A quad's `driverStand` (UTV and Land Rover, from `UTV_STAND.driver` and `LANDY_STAND.driver`, 1.35 and 1.45 m out on the right) makes the girls use `stepIn` through that door, sit as on the bench (`haySeatOffset`, legs together), and get out the same way. The grips are the steering wheel's, so the tractor's hand pose does the rest. Start and stand points are the door too.
- **Starting.** Anything with `compressionIgnition` (tractor, UTV) uses `TractorStartAttempt`, in the `starting` state too, so "Start it" works for them standing beside. The Land Rover on a flat battery (`needsHandle`) sends her to the front first (`cranking` state, `npc/crankAttempt.js`, pure, tested): key on, choke from cold, hold the handle 0.4 to 0.8 s, let go to swing, up to 10 swings, 1.2 s between; Jess is sloppy a quarter of the time. It drives `helperPull` and `helperGrip` on the quad, and a kick-back from her sets `pullByHer` so the player's "Ow" message and the nearest girl's `landyKick` stay for the player's own kicks. Measured in one headless run each: Jess didn't catch it in six swings (she was kicked once and sloppy twice, hence the limit going from 6 to 10); Amy caught it on the eighth.
- **Gears.** `NoviceShifter.grinds` plus `grindsGear()`: a change into a crash gear above `crashSpeed` while moving counts a `crunch` that the game turns into lines; the gear still engages. Amy's `AutoShifter` never does. The Land Rover also gets Jess's novice shifter (kangaroo hops, second at most, stalls as the tractor's).
- **The other girl** joins with `joinChance` 0.5 (under "Tractor go") when the driver starts mounting, after 0.5 to 2 s, through the existing bench slots; the driver waits for her as on the tractor (`friendOnBack` already counts anyone on the mount). She is "asked" so the UTV/Land Rover rider rules leave her be, and climbs out when the go ends.
- **Lines** (`vehicleGoLines.js`): ask, set off, done and watch for each vehicle, refusals for Amy, and the handle: swing, kick, kick watch, no luck; the crunch pair. Stalls and hops use the tractor's and the usual drive lines.
- **Debug and console.** "UTV go drive", "Land Rover go drive" (same ranges as the tractor's) and "Land Rover go handle" in Tune; the Girls tab's and the console's `drive` verb now go through `startGo` for these three, and there is a new `start` verb.
- **Checked:** unit tests (scheduler, crank attempt, crunch rule, door geometry, handle grip); headless: Jess drove the UTV from the yard (key, glow, crank, 3 to 5 m/s for about a minute and a half, parked, got out), Amy started the UTV by "Start it", Amy swung the Land Rover's handle on a flat battery, mounted and drove it, and the game loads without errors. **Not checked:** how the step-in and sitting at the wheel look from close up, Jess's go at the Land Rover with a charged battery, a crunch actually happening, the other girl joining, reverse on the UTV, her avoiding things in a vehicle this wide, how often goes happen in real play, the lines by ear (re-recording started).


## New models for the girls, a viewer test (parked, 2026-10-08)

Jamie asked whether better models would fix the girls' clothes, which never came out right; the test answered yes, and the work is parked until there's a free body of the right build.

- **Why the clothes kept going wrong.** Both girls are the one Quaternius "Animated Woman" (1,908 triangles, flat shaded, one palette texture). The polo is colour painted on the body, every garment is her mesh pushed out, and a single triangle runs from her shoulder nearly to her jaw, so no neckline or collar has anything to sit on. `outfit.js` (816 lines, 18 commits) mostly works round that.
- **What was tried.** Quaternius's Universal Base Characters (CC0), free tier, in a side-by-side viewer with the game's own Amy (`.scratch/girls/viewer.html`, untracked; screenshots by `.scratch/pp/girlview.mjs`, own dev server on 5181). The female body is about 12,800 triangles with separate eyes, eyebrows and hair, and a 65-joint UE5-style skeleton with full fingers. The free Universal Animation Library (CC0, 43 clips) is on the same skeleton, so nothing needs retargeting: Idle_Loop, Walk_Loop, Jog_Fwd_Loop, Sitting_Enter/Idle_Loop/Exit, Driving_Loop, PickUp_Table, Fixing_Kneeling, Push_Loop, Idle_Talking_Loop and Interact cover nearly everything the game plays.
- **What it showed.** A rough polo and shorts (her own triangles chosen by skin weight and bind-pose height, pushed out along smoothed normals, skinned to the same skeleton) gave a clean neckline at the base of her neck first time, and a smooth fit over the bust and shoulders. Hair and eyebrows bound to the body's bones by name and followed the clips.
- **Why it's parked.** The free tier has only the "Superhero" build (broad muscular shoulders, abs, fists clenched at idle), wrong for Amy and Jess, and reshaping it in code would be fighting the base model again. The "Regular" female is in the $19.99 Source tier, which Jamie doesn't want to buy yet. Alternatives: MakeHuman through MPFB2 (CC0, realistic, separate faces and clothes, but needs Blender, which would be driven by script), or another CC0 body if one turns up. Ruled out: Reallusion's free bases (not CC0), AI-generated meshes (poor topology and rigs).
- **Gotchas for whoever picks it up.** The pack's COLOR_0 vertex colours are shader masks, so turn `vertexColors` off or she renders dark; the "Godot - UE" folder's normal maps are DirectX convention, so use the "Normals Unity - Godot" ones; the hair textures are grey, meant to be tinted; the skin texture is tanned, and a material colour can only darken it; a smooth body needs the sun's shadow bias (the game's sky already has it) or it shows fine stripes of shadow acne. The pack zips are in `.scratch/girls/`.
- **If it goes ahead.** Swap behind `?girls=new`: a bone-name map (our Mixamo-style names to `upperarm_l` and the rest), the library's clips under the names the game plays, garments as skinned shells with drape, then delete the old outfit workarounds.

- **Afterwards, from Jamie:** the Land Rover turned slightly on the spot with nobody in it, and the debug panel had no choke for it. The first was two things: a girl getting out didn't always set the handbrake (she does now on the UTV and the Land Rover), so it crept on its slope; and a parked quad's drift was only cancelled in a straight line, so the wheels-locked chassis jittered in yaw (a parked quad turning under 0.6 rad/s is now stopped; measured ±0.4 rad/s before and 0 after on the Land Rover). The choke row was hidden for anything with no fuel tap, and the Land Rover has a choke and no tap; it shows, and can be set, in the Vehicles tab and the console now.

Still no ride offers from the girls while you sit there. Not offered on a moving one (the quads' "she pulls up and moves for you" is left off these).

While you are aboard the UTV or Land Rover doesn't count as parked or free, so nobody climbs in by herself and no new go starts with you sat in it.

## The girls see the trailers (TODO 14)

- Cause: the trailers follow the hitch and have no physics body, so the girls' ray probes (on foot in `Companion.blocked`, driving in `driveProbe` and `driveClear`) went straight through them.
- Fix: each trailer gets a kinematic box (`src/vehicle/trailerObstacle.js`), moved with it every frame: the bed or tub footprint, 0.2 to 1.0 m above the axle's ground so a ray at knee height hits it between the wheels. Like the girls' own colliders it has solver groups `0xffff0000`, so it makes no contact forces: it can't shove a quad or the tractor towing it, and nothing is stopped by it. Only rays see it (and the player's character controller, so you can't walk through one).
- A girl driving ignores the trailer she is towing (`towedBy`), otherwise her reverse probe would hit it and she would back off for ever.
- Not done: the drawbar and swing of a trailer behind her (she sees only the bed, not the arc it sweeps on a tight turn), and a quad you drive into a trailer still passes through. Walking-round-vehicles avoidance is unchanged beyond the trailers; no new logic for the quads, which her probes already saw.
- Tests: `tests/trailerObstacle.test.js` (box placement, which trailer is ignored, and in Rapier that a ray hits it and an overlapping quad isn't moved). **Not checked:** in the running game.

### Walking round vehicles, a better way (follow-up to TODO 14)

- Cause of the bumps: on foot she looked along one ray down her middle for 1.4 m, so a shoulder clipped any corner the ray missed, and the turn back toward her target began the moment the line looked clear.
- `Companion.blocked` now casts three rays (her middle and 0.4 m out either side, `probeWidth`) when looking more than a metre; close to a destination it is the middle only, so she can still walk up beside a quad to get on. `probeDistance` went from 1.4 to 2.2 m, and the angles tried either side are finer (0.4 rad steps, out to 2.5).
- Once she finds a way round she keeps that heading for `avoidHold` (2 s) while it stays clear, rather than turning back in at the first gap. Both are in the "Moving about" tuning.
- Not tested in unit tests (it needs a whole companion in Rapier); **not checked in the running game**, so the numbers are first guesses.

## Girls stay full size in the Land Rover

- **The cause.** Both Land Rovers (and the UTV) are drawn at 1.15 times, and the model's scale passes down to anything hung under it. The quads already cancelled that on their seat anchors (`1 / scale`), but the bench seats (`seatLeft`, `seatMid`) and the UTV's `bedSeat` were left out, so a girl in the back or on the bench came out 15% taller than on the ground.
- **The fix.** `src/vehicle/unscaleAnchors.js` holds one list of every seat anchor and undoes the scale on each; `quad.js` calls it. A new seat anchor goes on that list. The model's scale stays, since it sizes the vehicle to the girls.

## Sheltering in a cab (TODO 23)

- `src/npc/cabShelter.js` (pure, tested) decides; `game.js` (`updateCabShelter`, `handleCabEvent`) acts, using the existing bench riding (`boardTrailer`, `UtvRide.ask`) and `requestStart`. No new girl state: she is "going" (walking over, or starting it first) and then "in" (aboard on the bench), and the existing hay-ride states do the climbing.
- Rain: when the rain sets in, each girl rolls once for the spell (`rainChance` 0.5) whether she'll use a cab instead of the barn, with a delay of 2 to 8 s (the barn's starts at 4, so the cab is picked first; a girl who is already heading for a cab is busy to the barn logic). The nearest open cab within `cabRange` 45 m with a free bench seat: the UTV (roof and cage) or either Land Rover. She starts a Land Rover that is not running 35% of the time (`rainStartChance`); the UTV has no heater, so she never starts it.
- Cold: on a dry day with warmth under `coldBelow` 0.5 (overcast, fog; the same scale as warming their hands), every 3 s a free girl near a Land Rover has `coldChance` 0.06 to go (doubled if it is already running). She starts it if it is not running (the starting handle on a flat battery, as when asked), and gives up after `goTimeout` 90 s if she cannot get in. Land Rovers only.
- Out: a cold-day girl stays 60 to 170 s and then gets out while it is still cold; a rain girl stays at least 40 to 120 s and then gets out 6 to 22 s after the rain has dropped under `dryBelow` 0.08. She also goes if the ride's own bored timer (120 to 300 s) gets her first, or if you take the vehicle. An engine she started is switched off when none of the girls who came for it is left, unless you are on it. Cooldown 150 s before she does it again.
- She has to be stood about and not resting, warming her hands, on a favour or a diesel job, so it does not fight the other stand-about behaviours. A cab with a girl driving, mounting or cranking it, or with you in it, or the UTV with its bed up, is not open.
- Debug: "Shelter in a cab" in Tune (Girls: moving about); each girl's folder has "gets into the nearest cab" and "starts a Land Rover and sits in it" (console `girl <name> shelter` and `warm-up`, both through `game.sendToCab`, which skips the weather and the dice).
- Lines: `cabRainGo`, `cabColdGo`, `cabIn`, `cabOut` for each girl (`cabShelterLines.js`).
- Not checked in the running game: how the walk, step-in and sitting look in the rain, how often it happens against the barn in a real shower, or the lines by ear. Unit tests cover the decisions, seat counts, give-up, switch-off and the lines.

## A game without the girls

Asked for by Jamie: a URL switch that leaves the girls out, with a name that means nothing so it can sit in the address bar without saying what it does. It is `?vx=2` (exactly that value; `src/npc/leftOut.js`, tested), and it is kept out of the README and the FAQ on purpose.

- Nobody is built rather than built and hidden: `game.companions` is empty, so everything that loops over the girls has nothing to do, and autonomy is switched off so nothing that waits on a girl starts. The per-girl ledgers (favour, skills, falling out, romance) are still made for both, untouched, so code that reads them by name still works. Sitting on the back waiting for a ride (`updateRideOffer`) was the one place that needed the first girl and now just returns.
- Checked headless on three seeds (one cold and wet): no girls, no errors, riding every vehicle there, sitting on a back, and every debug tab. Not checked: the console's `girl` commands, or every wheel and scripted moment by hand.

## Keeping a sick one alive (TODO 119)

Decided with Jamie (all as recommended, with two changes): she mostly does not switch it off when she gives up, and "sick" covers more than a hard start, so heavy wear and a creeping choke count too.

- `src/npc/keepAlive.js` (pure, tested). `sickness` scores a quad from what the girls can tell: the goes the last start took (`quad.startTrouble`, set when a girl's start finishes and cleared once it stops), plug fouling, a weak battery, heavy wear, the choke-creeps quirk and erratic running. Each cause is worth a score; `sickFrom` 0.5, so a hard start, wear over 0.5 or a creeping choke is enough alone, and fouling (0.35) or a weak battery (0.35) need a second cause.
- `KeepAlive` is the hand on the throttle. While it is running, sick and unattended she gives it `holdBase` 0.1 plus whatever gets it to `fastIdle` 1.5 times its idle, and a full handful for `catchHold` 0.4 s when the revs sag under `falterBelow` 0.85 of idle (not more often than every `catchGap` 3 s). She goes through the quad's `helperThrottle`, the way the revving and prank features do, so the engine, smoke and shake follow. Those features now leave a quad alone while she holds it.
- Who and when: the girl who last had the quad (the left-running record) walks over and sits on the back when it is sick, running and unattended and she is free within `goRange` 14 m (`goChance` 0.5 a second). She stays until you come or `giveUpAfter` 180 s pass, then gets off and does not do it again for `cooldown` 240 s. When she gives up she switches it off only `keyOffChance` 0.15 of the time; otherwise she leaves it running to its luck.
- Letting go: if you take the quad (or she gets off) after holding it for `letGoAfter` 4 s, a sick one dies with chance `letGoStall` 0.6 times its score, and she says so. If it dies under her she says so too.
- Her reasons: `keepSit` is the practical one. `keepTruth` is the buzz: Jess says it once after `truthAfterJess` 40 s; Amy only after `truthAfterAmy` 90 s with you within `truthRange` 4 m. The extra shaking on the back is item 124's, not built here; the girls' buzz tally is untouched.
- Panel and console: Running shows how sick it is and why; Problems has "hard start (goes it took)" (cleared by "Put it right", with a test); `hardStart` under `engineHealth` in the console; the Girls tab and `girl <name> keep-alive` send a girl to the picked vehicle (it makes the quad count as hard to start first). Tuning folder "Keeping it alive".
- Checked headless once: a forced go had Jess walk over, sit on the red quad and hold it at about 1,680 rpm with a throttle of about 0.11 for 24 s, with no page errors. Not checked: how it looks (the hand on the throttle has no pose of its own, so she just sits as on any back seat), the lines in play, or whether the numbers feel right over a real game.

A knock that does not do it takes `knockCut` 40 s off the sulk. `pushMax` 3 knocks in a row, each within `knockGap` 2.5 s of the last, is pushing it: `sulkPushed` and `pushExtra` 120 s on. Talked round, she says `sulkRound` and gets out; out by time she says `sulkOut`.

## Ready and waiting (TODO 123)

The option "I'll be a minute" is on the wheel whenever she is stood about and not already doing it. The quad is the one you ride most (`headStart.usual()`), else her favourite, else the nearest with a free back seat she may ride (`readyQuadFor`). She starts it with the ordinary routine if it is off (so she can flood it or give up), then mounts the pillion seat and stays (`seatedByChoice` off, so she is not bored off it).
- Waiting: she revs it every `revMin`/`revMax` 18 to 38 s (the quad's `helperThrottle` for `revSeconds` 0.7 s). The horn starts at `tootFromJess` 40 s and `tootFromAmy` 75 s, then every `tootGap` 28 s, shortening by `tootRamp` 0.82 each time to a floor of `tootGapMin` 9 s; short toots (`tootLength`, the horns' own 0.22 s) and from the `tootsLong` 3rd on long ones of 0.7 s; she says `readyToot` on the first and `readyImpatient` on the `impatientAt` 2nd. `Horns.toot` is new and shares the answering beeps' machinery, so the quad's horn, the wheel pad and the sound all follow.
- You come back (within `doneRange` 2.5 m, or on that quad): `readyHere`, and she is a normal pillion. Too long (`giveUpJess` 150 s, `giveUpAmy` 210 s): `readyGiveUp`, switching it off `keyOffChance` 0.5 of the time, and she gets off. If it dies under her, or she never gets going in `goTimeout` 150 s, it just ends.
- While she waits nothing else revs her quad or switches it off: `holdingIdle` on the companion (the same flag item 119's hold uses) is also true while `readyHolding` is set, and the rev-off, prank, drown-out and key-off checks already stand aside for it.
- A cold engine: she keeps a little choke and 0.15 throttle on until it is half warm, once she has stopped starting it and while she walks over and sits (without it the engine died at 298 rpm before she got there, in a headless run). She stops tending it at 0.3 temperature, lower than the cab shelter's 0.5.
- Debug: "Ready and waiting" in Tune (Girls: how they feel about you); each girl's folder has "gets a quad going and waits on the back for you" (console `girl <name> wait-ready`). Lines: `readyGo`, `readyToot`, `readyImpatient`, `readyGiveUp`, `readyHere` for each girl (`readyLines.js`).
- Checked headless once, with the toot times shortened: Jess started the red quad, sat on the back with it running at a held throttle, and the horn toots counted up, with no page errors. Not checked: how it looks and sounds (the revs, the horn, her on the seat), the lines by ear, or the real-time pacing (the stretch to the first toot, the give-up times) in a real game.

## Buzz-seeking requests (TODO 127)

Asked as the others are: you on foot, close, free girl, after the cooldown.

Starting the tractor with her request open sends her up at once (`wants` in `mudguardRide.js`'s idle board, only once it is running), instead of waiting for the slow idle chance.
- Her own choice: the new bored choice `buzz` (`boredBuzzChance`: Jess 0.14, Amy 0.03; `buzzWear` 0.3, the engine wear from which a quad counts as rough). The quads near her with a free back and wear over that are the options, the roughest first. She starts it first if it is off, then sits on the back (`requestBuzzSit`) and stays a while like the other sit-on-it choices. Jess says `selfBuzz` openly ("This one shakes properly now. Mine."); Amy's `selfBuzz` is neutral ("I think I will sit on this one for a bit"), since she never admits it. Item 126's favourite-follows-the-buzz already makes the rough one her favourite, and now she goes to it.

## Numb (TODO 128)

Numbness builds while she sits on a seat that shakes at least `shakeFrom` 0.4 on the same body-shake level the buzz counts (a warm idle is about 0.6, a cold one about 1.2), at `level / shakeFull` (capped at `shakeCap` 2) over `fullAfter` 150 s: about two and a half minutes at level 1, a bit over four at a warm idle's 0.6, 75 s at the cap. It tingles at `tingleAt` 0.65 of the way, and is numb at 1, then resets. It drains at `drainPerSecond` 0.05 while she is not sitting on it, so a short sit leaves nothing.
- Which sits count (`companionNumb.js`): on the back of a quad of her own choice (`seatedByChoice`: the bored sit and the buzz-seeking sit of item 127), parked with the engine running and nobody riding. Not holding it for you (`holdingIdle`: item 119's hold, waiting for you, warming it up), and not while you ride with her. Those have their own reasons to stay put.

She says `numbTingle`, `numbGo`, `numbStretch` and `numbBack`. The voice now keeps `level` (the shake it already measured) for this.

When both girls are free and within `together` 7 m, the game has been going `startAfter` 240 s, each has felt at least `minTotal` 60 shake-seconds across the quads, nobody else is talking and `cooldown` 300 s has passed, they have `chance` 0.015 a second to compare. `ranking` orders the quads by a tally, leaving out any she has felt nothing on, and only the quads in this game.

A girl who cannot (a seat taken, a quad she may not ride) just stays, and the verdicts are said anyway.
