## The polo as a garment (spike, behind `?polo=shell`)

Jamie asked whether the polo could be built on top of her body like the jumper instead of painted on. Painted, it can only change colour where the body's triangles allow, and one triangle runs from her shoulder nearly to her jaw. A shell of her own triangles (as the jumper is) has the same limit: it either keeps that triangle (a roll neck) or drops it (a scoop on her shoulders). The way through is to cut the shell's triangles along a smooth line, adding vertices of its own, so its edges are where they should be however coarse the body is.

Settled with Jamie before building:

1. Behind a flag, `?polo=shell`, compared side by side with the painted polo before the painted one goes.
2. Collarless first and get that right, then a collar. Fairly detailed: a placket at least as long as now, better buttons.
3. The hem tucked into the shorts.
4. Short sleeves.

What was found first, and matters for every garment: **her body mesh is stored in a different pose from the one she stands in.** The positions in the mesh (which `skinnedGarment`'s `wear`, `offset` and `shape` get, and which `cutNeckline` measures) are a T-pose turned a quarter round: her front is +x and her left is -z, about 2 cm higher. Her bones are measured as she stands: front +z, left +x, arms down. Every rule that mixes the two (an angle round her neck, "in front of her", distance to her hand) is a quarter turn out. The painted neckline's dip and the jumper's scoop neck are on her right side, not her front, and the jumper's fall from the bust is worked out across her side. With the painted polo this hid itself, because what showed through the jumper's misplaced scoop was shirt-coloured body. It likely explains a good part of the collar's troubles.

- **The pipeline** (`src/npc/garmentMesh.js`, pure, tested): each body vertex carries both positions (`bind` as stored, `rest` as she stands) and `skin`, the 3x4 map from one to the other (her skinning matrices at rest; checked against the posed positions to 4 micrometres). Decisions are made on `rest`; the garment is built on `bind`, which is what gets skinned. A shape worked out as she stands (the fall from the bust, the placket) is carried back through the inverse of the skin. Steps: weld the flat-shaded corners into one vertex with an averaged normal; subdivide once, the new points pushed out over a surface through the corners' normals (Phong tessellation) so the cloth is rounder than her body; cut along fields (below 0 is inside), splitting triangles exactly on the line with new vertices whose weights are blended (strongest four, summing to 1); draw band lines in with both sides kept and their own copies of the points, so a colour changes sharply; smooth normals by position so the copies shade as one surface.
- **The tops** (`src/npc/polo.js`): one builder, `buildTop`, with `POLO` and `JUMPER` as its settings. The neck is a line round her neck (`neckBack`, `neckSide`, `neckFront` above the neck bone at `neckRing` out, lower by `neckSlope` per metre further out) capped at `neckCap` 4 cm above the neck bone. Without the cap, her jaw (wide, and far from the middle of her neck) counted as inside and grew a patch of shirt under her chin, which looked like shirt-coloured lips. Her neck base is a flat ring (the top of the model's old roll neck), so the line is set by how far out it sits: about 5 cm at the sides and back, 6 cm at the front where the placket starts. Sleeves are cut square across the arm, blended by how much that part of the arm carries the vertex, so her side by the armpit is never cut. The hem is a height, measured as the shorts measure theirs.
- **The polo**: 7 mm off her body, 5 mm on the sleeves flaring to 8 mm at the hem, 66% of the way from shoulder to elbow (her upper arm has only two rings of vertices, and the painted sleeve stops at 56%, so the cut runs through one long band of triangles and covers it). A 1.1 cm neck band and 1.6 cm cuffs 16% darker, turned in so the edges have thickness. The hem is 3.5 cm below the top of the shorts, drawn in to 2.5 mm below their waistband (not rounded out there, since the shorts are her flat faces pushed out) and blousing 5 mm over it. Below the bust the front falls back at 0.32 m per m instead of following her in. Built in about 20 ms, about 1,800 triangles.
- **The placket**: found by rays at the finished shirt as she stands, 13.5 cm long (12 before) and 2.4 cm wide, 2.2 mm proud with its long edges turned down, flat across (it bridges any dip between her breasts). A stitch texture (tonal thread down both edges and a box across the bottom, drawn once). Three buttons, each a turned profile with a raised rim and a dished face, and four holes in the shirt's colour, darkened. The fabric matches the shirt: the texture's ground is divided out in linear colour (dividing by its sRGB value left it darker than the shirt).
- **The body underneath** stays shirt-coloured, so anything poking through in an odd pose is shirt on shirt. Its neckline paint (`cutNeckline`, given the garment's line) sits 6 mm inside the garment's edge, so no shirt colour shows above it.
- **The jumper** goes over it on the same pipeline when the flag is on: 2.6 cm off, to 86% of the forearm, ribbed cuffs drawn in to 9 mm, a hem 9 cm below the shorts' top (11 cm caught the tops of her thighs, whose triangles belong to her legs) with a 3 cm rib, neck band 2.2 cm, everything 24% darker at the bands, falling straight from the bust at 0.18 m per m. The old jumper over the new polo let the polo through at her right shoulder (its scoop) and under the bust; this one covers it, with its neck showing just the polo's band. With the flag off, the old jumper and painted polo are untouched.
- **Measured**: load time with and without the flag, alternated, 2.10 and 2.80 s against 2.15 and 3.06 s (no difference beyond noise). `npm test` passes; `tests/garmentMesh.test.js` covers the skin maps, welding, subdivision, cuts, rays, and the polo and jumper built on a stand-in body stored turned a quarter round, so a mix-up between the two frames fails.
- **Checked headless** at full size, both girls in a mustard shirt and Amy in her seed's dark green: front, close on the neck, three-quarter, side, back, shoulder, full length; Amy sitting on a quad from the side, front and behind, and driving one with her arms forward on the bars; both in jumpers; and the same shots with the flag off for comparison. Not checked: the shirt in motion (walking, mounting), Jess sitting or driving.
- **Not done yet**: the collar (next, built from the neck's edge loop, which the cut leaves as an ordered ring of vertices with known weights); the shorts' waistband, which is still whole triangles and dips in a V at her back; tunables in the debug panel (the settings are plain objects for now, to register once the painted polo goes); fixing the old jumper's quarter turn with the flag off (it goes when the garment version replaces it).

### Follow-up: more buttons, Jess's open neck, tucked in, the jumper's neck

Jamie: more buttons and a slightly deeper placket with the top one nearer the neck; try Jess with her top button open, before the collar; the polo tucked fully into the waist; the jumper's neck very slightly deeper.

- **Placket**: 15.5 cm with five buttons 3.2 cm apart, the top one 1 cm below the neckline (`buttons` are now distances down it). At 17.5 cm the last button sat on the crease under the bust. Buttons sit on the placket's own face, which bridges the dip between her breasts; on the shirt under it they sank in and showed as crescents.
- **Jess's open neck** (`poloOpen: 1` in her outfit, `open` in the settings): the neck cut gets a V at her front, from the round neckline down to 9 mm above her second button, 1.7 cm either side at the top (`openNeck`, measured on the rounded shirt before it's cut; its field is only on her front, so her back is never cut). Above the V's point the placket is two half-strips lying along its edges, each with its own turned-down sides; the undone button is on her left half and a buttonhole slit on her right. Inside the V is her skin: the body's paint can't follow a V across its coarse triangles (a triangle spanning the V is painted shirt all across), so a patch of her own flat-shaded triangles cut to the V and lifted 0.8 mm covers it, in her skin colour (`buildInset`). Lifting each face along its own normal opened cracks at the creases that showed the shirt paint; each corner is lifted along the average of its faces, still drawn flat.
- **Tucked in**: the shorts were her whole triangles, so their top dipped in a V at her back and the polo showed down into it. With the flag on they are cut the new way too (`src/npc/shorts.js`): level at the waist, square across each thigh at 56% to the knee, a 3 cm waistband a little darker, turned-in edges, 6 mm off at the waist flaring 12 mm towards the hem. The waist and the polo's hem are level as she stands, not in the stored pose (which dipped 1 to 2 cm at her back), at the same height as the old shorts' top. The polo is drawn in to 2.5 mm from 2.5 cm above the waist down, so it is inside the shorts all the way to their top (it reached its tuck only below the waistband before, standing level with them right at the top). A test checks every polo point below the waist is nearer her than every point of the shorts.
- **The shorts aren't rounded**: rounded like the polo, they tore open across her lap when she sat on a quad. A point between two of her vertices, given their weights blended, doesn't stay between them once skinned at a joint bent as far as her hips are sitting; at the inside of the bend it sinks and her body comes through. Her own vertices bend exactly as she does. (The polo's rounding hasn't shown this at the shoulders or waist in the poses looked at.)
- **The jumper's neck** is 6 mm lower at the front and 3 mm at the sides and back: it shows Amy's top button and the top of Jess's V.
- Checked headless at full size: both girls front, neck, three-quarter, side, back and full length; Jess driving from the front and side; Amy on the back of a quad from behind; both in jumpers. Not checked: the shirt in motion, Amy driving since this change.

### Follow-up: Jess further open, the seat of the shorts, tonal buttons, and the collar

Jamie: open Jess's shirt more, 3 or 4 buttons, with a tiny visible cleavage, and keep 1, 2 and 3 open as designs to adjust or make random; the hips blend into the shorts badly (a visible bottom, then straight in); buttons a shade darker than the shirt, as in real life; then the collars, straight away.

- **Buttons undone**, 0 to 4, each a V of its own (`veeWidths`: half-widths at the neckline 1.7, 2.2, 2.7 and 3.1 cm, down to 9 mm above the first button done up). Jess has 4: the V reaches the top of her bust and shows a little of it. It's per girl (`poloOpen` in her outfit) and settable live: "polo buttons undone" in her folder on the Girls tab, `girls.jess.poloOpen` in the console (`Companion.setPoloOpen`, which refits her clothes). Not random yet.
- **Cutting a V exactly.** The V's point is narrower than the shirt's triangles, so a triangle straddling her middle couldn't be cut along both sides of it, and the shirt and the skin inside covered the lower part of the V. Both are split down her middle first (`cut` with `keep: 'split'`: the line drawn in, nothing thrown away, the points shared); either side of it the V's edge is a straight line, which a cut follows exactly. The skin inside is also split flat (each triangle into four in its own plane, so it still matches her facets) and picked generously, since the V's point can lie inside one of her triangles without touching a corner. The turned-in edge is left off down the V: the placket halves cover that edge, and turned in it filled the opening.
- **The seat.** The shorts clung to her bottom and went straight in under it. Below the fullest part of her bottom (its furthest-back point in each 2 cm across, the widest of five so it bridges between the two sides) they now fall towards the hem at 0.12 m forward per m down, up to 6 cm off her, fading out towards her sides and her front. 0.22 and 0.3 hardly showed: her bottom's fullest point is high, so at those slopes the fall had come back in by the hem. The shorts are still her own coarse triangles (rounded, they tore across her lap when sitting), so the seat has some facets.
- **Buttons** are the shirt's colour at 72% (`buttonShade`), their holes and the buttonholes darker still.
- **The collar** (`src/npc/collar.js`, pure, tested). The earlier attempts were fitted to her body, which has no neckline to sit on; this one stands on the garment polo's cut neck edge, a smooth ring of points, interpolated round her neck by angle. A stand 2.1 cm high at the back and 1.6 at the front ends, leaning in 3 mm; the fold rolls out 9 mm over its top; the fall comes out 2.6 cm and down to 1 cm below the neckline, more at the points (2.2 cm further out and 3.8 cm further down, turned towards her front, over the last 0.9 rad at each end), the corners rounded. Done up, its two ends meet over the placket (Jamie asked for a collar buttoned up and closed at the front before); on an open neck they're at the top corners of the V, so the points splay. The fall is laid on whatever is outermost by rays from a point inside her chest (the shirt, or the jumper when she has one on, so the collar lies out over the jumper's neck), 2.5 mm off it, and its skin blends from the neck edge's to whatever it lies on, so it moves with her shoulders. Laid point by point, neighbours lifted by different amounts folded a triangle over and showed its dark back; three passes evening the fall out, each followed by lifting anything that went under back out, fixed that. It's a slab 1.6 mm thick with every edge closed, thinned to a third over the fold (at full thickness the inside showed through the sharp bend); the outer edge 10% darker and the inside 28%.
- **Order.** With the flag on, the polo is built, then the jumper, then the collar over both. About 1,300 points and 2,600 triangles, built in about 70 ms a girl. Every refit (her shirt colour at the start, a jumper on or off) rebuilds the lot, about 0.2 s a girl.
- **Measured**: load time with and without the flag, alternated, 2.30 and 2.31 s against 2.26 and 2.06 s, so up to about a quarter of a second more, inside the 2.5 s budget.
- Checked headless at full size: both girls' collars close up from the front, three-quarter and back; Jess with 3 and 4 undone; both in jumpers with the collar over; Amy driving; Jess's hips from the side, back and three-quarter back before and after the seat. Not checked: the clothes in motion, Jess driving since the collar.

### The garments made the default (2026-10-13)

Jamie: the orange polo was more of a red; take the flag away and make the garments the default, deleting the old code.

- **Colour.** The painted polo's colour was its hex's linear value written into the texture as if it were sRGB, which comes out darker and redder (that's why Amy's navy was once lightened to #3a5690). The garments carried that over to match the paint under them. They now use the colour as it is, the quad's own, so the orange quad's girl wears orange; the shirt band of the body's paint under them is written the same way (`recolourTexture`, the shirt band only: hair and shoes keep their old reading, which their colours were chosen for). The logo's thread turns dark on the lighter shirts this makes.
- **Deleted**: the flag (`?polo=shell`), the painted polo's placket and buttons (`addPoloFront`, `chestPoint`, `shirtFrontTop`), the old jumper (`addJumper`) and shorts (`addShorts`), `skinnedGarment` (only they used it), the old neckline (`necklineAt` and its constants, and `cutNeckline`'s own line: it now always takes the polo's), `shirtVertices` and `shirtColour`. The outfits' `collar` key is now `polo`. Kept: the wellies (`fittedShell`, rays at a posed copy of the body), the palette recolour and the neckline strip under the polo. The lane's visitors use the plain original outfit, and the neighbour's jumper is the garment jumper.
- **Fitted once.** Each girl's garments were built when she was made and again straight after, when her shirt colour and buttons are dealt. They're now only fitted then (`applyOutfit`'s `garments: false`, then `setShirt`, or `Companion.dress` for a girl with no favourite quad). Load to "Ready": 2.38 to 2.54 s (2.52 and 2.69 before, building twice), at about the 2.5 s budget.
- **Mud.** The mud and dust drawn on a girl (`girlMud`) are on her body's material, so under her polo they don't show now, as they never did under her shorts or wellies; her face and arms still show them. Mud on the clothes themselves is left for later.
- The FAQ's notes on the girls say their polos are the colours of their favourite quads (they said navy and burgundy, true only before the shirts matched the quads), with a new answer on why the shirts and buttons change between farms; the README likewise.
- Checked headless: both girls full length in their own colours (Amy green, Jess red on seed 3), both in the orange quad's orange, Jess's open neck in yellow, the game loading with no errors of its own.

### Follow-up: a crease over the shoulder when driving

Jamie: an artefact poking out of the rear shoulder on a quad, e.g. Jess with 2 or 3 undone. It was a crease in the polo over her left shoulder blade with her arms forward on the bars, for both girls and with any number undone; her body alone (the polo hidden) showed none. Ruled out first: a gap in the collar's neck ring (it has a 0.6 rad gap with nothing undone too), and the rounding (unrounded, a thinner crease stayed). The body's weights change abruptly there, between her back, shoulder and upper arm; with no thickness it doesn't matter, but the polo stands 5 to 7 mm off, and skinning carries that offset in quite different directions at neighbouring points once the shoulder bends, which makes a valley.

- `smoothSkinning` (`garmentMesh.js`, tested): each point's bone weights, and its skin map with them (it's linear in the weights, so rest positions stay right), move halfway towards its neighbours' average, four passes (`skinSmoothing`), right after the polo is rounded, so the placket, logo and collar, built on it afterwards, take the evened-out skinning too. Points at one spot are kept together.
- Restricting it (her back only, or not the sleeves, or not the sleeves' fronts) left the crease, the edge of the smoothed part making its own; smoothed everywhere it's gone. Counting triangles that turn over when driving: 2 before; 37 smoothed everywhere, at the front of her shoulders and the inside of her upper arms, where the arms coming forward squeeze the cloth. None of those showed in close shots of both armpits from the front, the sides and front three-quarter.
- Checked headless: both girls driving, from behind either side and straight behind, the front and both sides; Jess with 2 and 3 undone; standing from the front, three-quarter, back and shoulder, and Jess's open neck straight on. A shadow on Amy's upper back standing is her hair's (it's there before the change too). The jumper is built by the same code with the polo's settings under its own, so it's evened out too. ## Two different figures, a rounder bust, and the seat of the shorts (2026-10-14)

Jamie: the bust a little rounder and more shapely, wider, and growing in width and depth as it gets bigger; the girls with slightly different figures and heights; then the shorts showed a bump before going back in, and should blend into a shapely bottom.

- **The bust** (`figure.js`): its region reaches 18.5 cm-equivalent across (0.185 of her hips-to-neck length, was 0.16), swells more towards her sides (`sideSwell` 0.4, the rest 0.25), and is domed rounder (0.3). A bigger bust covers more of her as well as standing out further: over 1 its region is 45% wider and its push and depth 45% greater per unit (`grow`), so a full one stays round rather than a point on a narrow base.
- **The figures** (`characters.js`), settled from the shots: Amy the taller at 1.58 m (was 1.50) with the balanced figure (bust 1.0, waist 0.9, hips 0.95); Jess 1.52 m (was 1.53) and curvier (bust 1.4, waist 1.15, hips 1.2). All four are the Companion tuning sliders.
- **The seat.** Hiding the shorts showed the bump was her body: the model's bottom is one flat plate across both sides (the old painted shirt's back) ending in a shelf that steps 8 cm in to her thighs over 2 cm of height, and the shorts, her faces pushed out, copied it. Two things now:
  - Her body (`roundSeat` in `figure.js`, pure, tested) is shaped as two round cheeks 5 cm either side of her middle: each point behind her goes to the cheek's surface, fullest 40% of the way up the plate, reaching back as far as she did at her fullest and curving in so the plate's bottom edge comes in, up to 7 cm at her middle. The model has points only down her middle and about 9 cm either side, so the cheeks are wide enough that those all fall inside them (narrower, the hem's points sat on their edge and never moved). Smoothing her back profile up and down (tried first) hardly changed it: there are only three rows of points there.
  - The shorts (`seat` in `shorts.js`) fill out over the same two cheeks below the waistband, only ever further out, evened out between neighbours. Subdividing the shorts so the seat could curve more (flat, not rounded) was tried and dropped: sitting on a quad, her polo came through at her hips, as rounding had done before.
  - From the side and three-quarter behind it's a rounded bottom now; straight from behind a faint crease is still where the plate's edge was (her body's triangles).
- Checked headless: both girls side by side front, close and in profile; Jess's shorts from both sides, behind, three-quarter behind and low; Amy three-quarter behind; Amy sitting on a quad. Not checked: driving, the jumper or coat hem over the new seat.

### Follow-up: a fuller underside, and rounder as it grows

Jamie: Jess's bust dropped away too quickly underneath and still wasn't round enough as it got bigger.

- **Fuller underneath** (`figure.js`): the dome is measured from a fullest line `fullBelow` 0.03 (times its size) below the apex, so the lower half is the fuller one. Below that line the push holds out longer (`domeUnder` 0.16 against `dome` 0.3) and meets her body over a shorter edge (`foldEdge` 0.3 of the way in, against 0.5), tucking in at the fold; the change is blended in over a short way below the line, so there's no step.
- **Rounder as it grows**: below its fullest line its height now grows with its size as its width does (it grew half as fast before, so a big one was wide and shallow), and the dome's power is divided by the square root of its size, so a bigger one is rounder rather than flat-topped.
- Checked: unit tests (fuller below the apex than the same distance above; a bigger one reaching further down in more than proportion; no jumps). Not looked at in the game.

## The girls talk over each other and start in step (issue #2, was TODO 107)

- **Starting in step.** Both girls started with small needs, a first think within 2 s and the same think gap, so they chose at nearly the same moment and the first pick was nearly always a quad. Now each starts with random purpose, excitement, company and rest, and stands for `firstThink` (1.5 to 7.5 times `thinkEvery`, 3 to 15 s) before her first think.
- **Thinking.** The gap between thoughts is `nextThink` (chooser.js): log-normal around `thinkEvery`, never under a fifth of it, so some thoughts come quick and some are mulled over. Sleepiness still slows it.
- **Talking over.** The lines she says as she sets off (`watchOwnMind`) were `urgent`, which skips the shared floor, so both girls could announce a plan at once. They now pass `waitTurn`: no quiet gap, but if the other girl is still talking the line is dropped and she just goes. Reactions to events stay urgent.

## Calling you to help (issue #3, was TODO 108)

Settled with Jamie before building: bog and tip now (running dry is item 40's, and keepAlive already covers some of it); she stays up on a bogged quad, standing on the footboards, and climbs out beside a tipped one; she names where she is from a list of spots; she shouts when you are within about 80 m and less often beyond, and the map rings her; winch, chain or a shove frees her; favour +2 (up to +1 more the sooner), -1.5 when left, and after about 4 minutes she gives up.

- **Pure parts.** `src/npc/stranded.js` (`Stranded`, tested in `tests/stranded.test.js`) keeps the waiting: first shout at 2.5 s, then every 14 s with you within `hearRange` 80 m and every 45 s beyond; `came` once you have been within 7 m of her quad; `rescued` when the quad is free (bog sink under 0.3, or upright again); `left` once at 180 s if you never came near (favour -1.5); `gaveUp` at 240 s (favour -0.5 if you never came, else nothing). A rescue is worth `rescueFavour` 2 plus up to `quickFavour` 1, easing to nothing by 120 s, and only if you came; a quad that frees itself with you nowhere near earns nothing. `src/npc/spotNames.js` is 21 named spots with coordinates (`nearestNamedSpot`); a spot's line says "in the yard", "by the ford", "by the Dutch barn" and so on. All the tunables are in "Girl calling for help" in the Tune tab.
- **Bogged.** `HerDrive.unstick` already gave up after a couple of goes; it now sets `strandedInBog`, and the driving state swaps her drive for `StrandedWait` (`strandedWait.js`) instead of ending it. That has the shape of `HerDrive` (as `TowSteer` does), keeps the brake off and the throttle shut, and works the throttle at `pushThrottle` 0.6 while the quad is `towed` (winch or chain) or being shoved. She is posed standing (`standOffset`, the seat's place with her feet where they hang; `standRaise` nudges it) with her right arm waving over her head (`startCalling` in `companionWaving.js`, the same poser as waving smoke away, with a high target and a slower swing). `companion.hold('stranded')` keeps the chooser and everything else off her.
- **Shoving.** Holding `V` by the quad already pushed a driverless quad. With a stranded girl at the controls the quad now calls `Bog.shove`: a bog sunk less than `pushLimit` 0.6 loosens by `pushRelief` 0.12 a second, so a typical stuck quad (0.3 to 0.55) is out in a few seconds, and a deep one (the winch's and chain's) does not move for a shove. Checked headless: sunk 0.51, shoved, under 0.3 in about 2 s and she got off.
- **Tipped.** The driving state used to end the drive at once when she tipped (`done:scared`) and get her off. It now sets `afterDismount` to `beginTipCall`: she stands by the quad waving (an idle girl with `waving.call`), shouts, and is rescued when you right it (`E`). If anything else takes her (you ask her to do something) the call just ends. `updateTipCall` runs in `Companion.update`.
- **Rescued or given up:** the drive ends with result `rescued` or `gaveUp`; both get her off and neither rolls the "drive it home" step (`HOME_RESULTS_SKIPPED`), so she does not drive straight back into the mud. She does not walk back to the yard after giving up; she stands by the quad. Not done.
- **Lines.** `strandedLines.js`: for each spot, a bogged and a tipped line per girl (`callout_bog_<id>`, `callout_tip_<id>`, the place written into the line, so they can be recorded), `calloutAgain` for the shouts between, thanks (quick or slow), left, and giving up. The first shout and every third say where she is. 49 new lines a girl, recorded. The generator's feel for the family comes from a prefix (`FEEL_PREFIX`).
- **Map.** The girl's marker gets a red ring and "(needs help)" while she calls.
- **Debug and console.** Girls tab, "Make her do": "bogs her quad" (sinks it where she is and makes the ground a bog until it is out, `Bog.fake`) and "tips her quad" (`Quad.tipOver`); the console's `girl <name> bog` and `girl <name> tip`. She has to be driving.
- **Checked:** unit tests; headless once, Amy on the red: bogged, she stood and waved, said where she was ("by the Dutch barn"), was shoved out and got off; tipped, she got off and waved by it, shouted, and on righting it earned favour 2.9 (came at once). **Not checked:** how the standing pose and the over-the-head wave look (no screenshot), where the feet sit on the footboards of each quad (`standRaise` is 0), Jess, a real winch or chain rescue with a girl aboard, the girls' friend commenting on a tip (the existing bog banter still covers a bog), and the lines in play.

## Driving lessons (issue #4, was TODO 109)

Settled with Jamie: the girls offer on their own through the chooser; one drill at a time, spoken, judged by what the drivetrain did; five drills for Amy; Jess has one ("faster"); what you have passed is kept; passing all of Amy's earns favour and an achievement.

- **Logic:** `src/npc/lessons.js` (pure, `tests/lessons.test.js`). `makeJudge(drill, params)` is a small state machine per drill, fed a sample of the drivetrain each frame (`running`, `gear`, `pedal`, `clutch`, `throttle`, `speed`, `climb`, `revs`; `stalled` is worked out from `running` going false). A go ends `good` or `bad` with a `why`. `Lessons` holds which drills each girl has passed, the active lesson and the cooldown.
- **Drills (Amy, in order):** `pullAway` (stopped in gear; from the pedal coming up to 2.5 m/s: bad if throttle goes over 0.45, revs over 0.55 of the range, or more than 3 s at the clutch's bite, or it stalls), `hillClutch` (same on a slope of 0.06 or more; bad if it rolls back over 0.4 m/s or slips 5 s), `changeDown` (the bank starts when the slope passes 0.08 coming off the flat; good if you are already in first or second, or changed down within 1.5 s of the foot of it; bad if you stayed up (`noChange`), changed later (`late`) or it is under 1.2 m/s after 5 s (`lugged`)), `smooth` (a change at over 2 m/s, from clutch down to a second after it is up: bad if you lost over 1.5 m/s or the revs went over 0.85), `stopping` (from over 4 m/s to a stop, bad if it stalls). Jess: `faster`, 11 m/s inside 8 s of a standing start with the throttle over 0.6, bad if you ease off (she does not mind a stall). All numbers are in `DEFAULT_LESSON_PARAMS`, the "Driving lessons" tuning group, and are first guesses.
- **Flow:** she offers (`OFFER_KINDS.lesson`, `fromSeat`, after 25 s of driving a manual together; Amy 0.6, Jess 0.45 weight; 240 s cooldown) then sets the first drill 3.5 s later. Two clean goes in a row pass it (a bad one resets the count), she moves to the next after 3.5 s. Four bad goes in a row, three reminders with no go at it (every 40 s), you getting off for 5 s or 15 minutes, and she gives up or says goodbye. The pillion girl is not "free" in the availability sense, so `updateMinds` treats a girl on the back of the manual you drive like one sat about: only `fromSeat` offers.
- **Rewards:** 0.5 favour a drill, 2 more for the last, and Amy's last gives the "Passed Amy's test" achievement. Passes are in `captureGirls`/`restoreGirls` (`lessons`), unknown drills ignored and a save without them loads.
- **Debug and console:** each girl's "Lessons" folder (passed, left, now, forget) and "starts a driving lesson" in "Make her do" (`girl amy lesson`); `lessonsPassed` in the console tree; "Driving lessons" tuning under the girls' feelings.
- **Lines:** `lessonLines.js` (offer, setting each drill, a reminder, a clean go, each way it goes wrong, a pass, done, giving up, goodbye). Amy has a few for Jess's drill because every situation exists for both. Needs recording.
- **Only on quads, for now:** the pillion seat is what lets a girl teach, so the tractor and Land Rovers are not covered; the bank and hill drills wait until you find one.
- **Not checked in the running game:** how the drills feel (the thresholds are first guesses), whether the offer comes at a good moment, and the lines by ear.

## Her voice shakes with the seat (issue #5, was TODO 110)

- **What:** while a girl is sat on a running vehicle her recorded voice is detuned and its volume moved by the seat's own heave (`unit.vibration.body.heave`, the signal that already shakes her model), so it follows the real shake: a slow thump on a cold single, a fast flutter on a two-stroke. Nothing new to record.
- **Logic:** `src/npc/voiceWobble.js` (pure, `tests/voiceWobble.test.js`) turns heave into `{ cents, gain }`. `CompanionVoice.watchSeat` feeds it each frame and `CompanionSounds.setWobble` applies it to the playing clip (`detune`, `setTargetAtTime` 8 ms, so a fine buzz is not smoothed away and nothing clicks) and to the next one. It resets to still when she is off or the engine is stopped.
- **Numbers (guesses, not judged by ear):** `voiceWobbleRef` 0.006 m of heave is a hard shake; `voiceWobbleCents` 45 of pitch swing and `voiceWobbleLevel` 0.22 of loudness at that, capped at twice the cents and 0.3 to 1.7 of the volume. A warm idle is 2 to 4 mm of heave, so it shows at about half strength; the green's 12 mm heave is clamped.
- **Jess does it on purpose:** her `voiceWobbleDepth` is 2.4 (Amy 1). They are companion params, so each girl's are in the debug panel with the rest of hers.
- **Not covered:** lines not yet recorded fall back to the browser's speech, which cannot be shaken. Not checked by ear.

## Waving (issue #6, was TODO 111)

- **What:** key `6` (`Digit6`, remappable, on the pad's radial menu) waves, on foot or from any seat. Each girl answers by how far off she is and what she is doing (`src/npc/playerWave.js`, pure, `tests/playerWave.test.js`): within `backRange` 30 m and stood about or walking she waves back (the existing `wave` reaction; Jess pulls a `face` instead half the time, which you will not see from where you stand); out to `callRange` 90 m a girl who is free to be asked (`generalRequests`, so soft jobs give way, the same test as the wheel's Come here) walks over; a girl in a plain drive pulls up for `driverWaits` 12 s and then carries on. Each girl answers once per `gap` 3 s.
- **Why 6:** every letter key is taken, and 5 is already the camera.
- **A driving girl waits, not stops:** `HerDrive.waveStop` holds her on the brakes in the `drive` phase and the wait does not count against the length of the drive, so she carries on afterwards. It does nothing in a race, doughnuts, a jump, showing off, tidying a quad, following or waiting for a passenger (`canWaveStop`). Asking her to stop from the wheel still ends the drive.
- **No new lines:** coming over and pulling up reuse the wheel's "come" and "stop" lines (`voice.onRequest`), so nothing needed recording.
- **Debug and console:** "Waving" tuning, "answers your wave" in each girl's "Make her do" (`girl amy answer-wave`), `waving.wave` in the console.
- **Not done:** you have no waving arm (first person; nothing is drawn). The girls being rarely free (the issue noted it) limits how often a wave brings one over; that is a separate problem. Not checked in the running game: how it feels, or the wave from a moving quad.

## Ringing the bell left a girl floating in a car (issue #245)

- **Cause (found by reading, not reproduced in the running game):** a girl in a cab (the UTV, the Land Rovers, the girls' Fiesta and Corsa) or on the tractor's arch is in the hay ride's `onTrailer` state, parented to the vehicle's seat. `Companion.recall` only knew how to get a girl off a quad (`seated`, `mounting`, `driving`, `resting`); for `onTrailer` and `boarding` it fell through to `walkTo`, so she set off walking in the vehicle's own frame: floating above the ground, moving with the car and never reaching the barn. "Come here" from the wheel (`requestComeHere`) had the same hole.
- **Fix:** `src/npc/recallPlan.js` (pure, `tests/recallPlan.test.js`) says the first step for every state, and `Companion.getDownThen` carries it out for both the bell and Come here: a girl on a seat climbs down by the way she got on (`leaveTrailer`, then walks once she is on the ground: `afterClimb`), one still climbing up goes straight back down (`leaveWhenUp`), one only walking over to board is stopped, and a fall off the trailer is left to finish (the bell nudges every 0.7 s). `game.leaveRides` tells the ride she was in (cab, arch, trailer) that she is leaving, so it does not send her back.
- **A guard so it cannot strand her again:** a girl stood or walking about whose root is still parented to a vehicle is dropped to the ground where she is, with a console warning (`Companion.dropIfStranded`).
- **Not checked:** the climb down from a cab in the running game, and whether the original sighting was this path; the issue's other suspect (getting out on the road on a car go, `carGo.js`) was not found to be a cause, but the guard would catch it.

## Asleep on the back (#7, was TODO 112)

- `src/npc/backNap.js` (pure, `tests/backNap.test.js`, tunables under "Asleep on the back"). One `BackNap` per girl (`companion.nap`), updated by `updateBackNaps` in game.js for the girl sat on the pillion seat of the quad you are riding. It is a drift that builds while the ride is calm, not a decision, so it is not an offer; it rewards riding well rather than fast.
- Calm is: you at the controls with the engine running, 3 to 14 m/s, along-nose acceleration under 1.2 m/s^2, the seat's vertical jerk under 3 m/s^2 (both from her own `PillionDynamics`), and the seat's shake level (the one `CompanionVoice` keeps for numbness) under 0.8. That shake level is what makes the engine matter: a cold lumpy single or a worn two-stroke never lets her drop off. A rough patch drains drowsiness twice as fast as it builds.
- Drowsiness fills in `dropSeconds` 45 of calm for an average girl, faster by her sleepiness need (up to 1.6 times) and her own `napProne` (Amy 1.3, Jess 0.7, in `characters.js`). She is asleep at 1. Her head follows, easing over 1.2 s.
- Waking: braking harder than 4.5 m/s^2, a bump over 9 m/s^2 or a shake of 2 wakes her with a start (`napStart`, the head snaps up in 0.35 s); the quad stopping for 3 s, or you talking to her (E), wakes her gently (`napGentle`). She does not drop off again for `gap` 120 s. Getting off clears it.
- Pose: `PillionPose.nap` (0..1) drops her head forward onto one shoulder (picked at random each time), leans her spine into you, eases the hold on your waist to half and stops the glances, looking back and attention. While asleep she says nothing unforced (`CompanionVoice.say`), does not grab the throttle, and snores through the existing `SnoreAudio`.
- Debug: the Girls tab's `nap` action (and `girl amy nap`) drops her off or wakes her; the tab shows who is drowsy.
- Thresholds are guesses from the numbers the voice wobble and numbness code use. Not checked in the running game: how the head and slack arms look from in front (look back), whether 45 s on a smooth lane is the right length, or whether the engines' shake levels land on either side of 0.8 and 2. Unit tests cover the drift, the wakes, the cooldown and the lines.

## She takes you to see something (#8, was TODO 113)

- Settled with Jamie: a sight-seeing flavour of the existing outing (TODO 105's, `outings.js`), not a new offer kind or timer, and only with you on the back (`quad.passenger`). Sights are the three that are always checkable from game state: a gate that is usually shut and is open (the road and grid gates, `gatesFoundOpen`; the south gate is tied open so never counts), the view (the foot of the hill, `hill.waitSpot`, looking 50 m up the pasture, because the girls will not take a quad past the foot, so the top itself is out of reach), and a quad running with nobody on it whose `LeftRunning.culprit` is known and which is not hers. The ruts Jess left need a record of where she drove, which is new state, so they are left for a later issue.
- `src/npc/sights.js` (pure, `tests/sights.test.js`): `sightsFrom` lists them from where she is (a gate or quad gets a stopping point `standOff` 7 m short on her side, the view its own), `pickSight` is `pickOuting` over them (nearer likelier, only by a way she can drive). `HerDrive`'s outing takes `sights` ({ p, pick, forced }): when it plans with you aboard it tries a sight first with `chance` 0.75, then falls back to the old outing roll, then loops. The trip carries `sight`; on arrival the drive pushes `sight:<kind>` (not `pause`), stops `stopMin..stopMax` 6 to 9 s, and the old return leg takes you back to where she started.
- At the place `showSight` (companionDriving.js) has her point (`startPointing`/`posePointing` in companionWaving.js: right arm out along the line to it, `pointSeconds` 3.5, with `attend` turning her head) and say the line. Gate and view lines are new (`sightLines.js`: `sightGo` setting off, `sightGate`, `sightView`); a quad left running reuses the `leftRunning*` lines, so she names whose it was.
- Debug: "Taking you to see something" in the Tune tab (Girls: things they do); a `sight` action in each girl's "Make her do" (and `girl <name> sight`), which forces the next drive to a sight with or without you on the back.
- Not checked in the running game: how the pointing arm looks from the seat behind her, or whether the view's stopping point (the ford) is a good place to look up from. Lines still to be recorded fall back to browser speech until the banks are rebuilt.

## Every frame threw after asleep on the back (#260)

- `c.nap.p = this.napParams` ran in the companion loop before `this.napParams` was made, so every nap had no params and `updateBackNaps` threw each frame, taking the rest of the farm's update (the chooser among it) with it. `napParams` is now made just before that loop.
- `tests/backNap.test.js` checks a default `BackNap` updates, and that game.js makes the params before handing them out (game.js can't be built without a browser). A guard so one feature's error can't stop the rest of the frame is a separate discussion.

## Practising on her own (#13, was TODO 118)

A girl who is unsure of the clutch goes and works at it on a parked manual, with nobody watching. The issue settled the behaviour (Jess sits on a parked manual, running, finding the bite point, stalling, restarting, blipping, trying the choke; skills go up and the stalls get rarer); I chose the shape and numbers.

- **An offer, `practise`** (`OFFER_KINDS`: purpose 0.7, excitement 0.2, brisk), from `src/npc/practising.js` (pure, tests in `tests/practising.test.js`). Its weight is her keenness (`weightJess` 1, `weightAmy` 0.25) times 1.2 minus her `starting` skill, so the worse she is the keener, and nothing for the first 90 s or for 420 s after a sitting. She needs a parked manual with a clutch of its own (`faults.hasClutch`: the three manual quads, not the tractor or the cars) within 40 m, nobody on it, with a free back seat.
- **She sits on the back, as for the warm-up** (`requestMount`, started first if it is off). A sitting is 70 to 130 s of: the bite (the clutch coming up over 1.6 s with a little throttle), a few blips, the choke tried for 5 s, with a pause between. Stall chance at the bite is `mistakeChance(0.45, starting)` (45% at 0.5, nearly none at 1, always at 0); after a stall she sits 1.4 s, then works the starter in 2 s bursts (choke on if cold) and gives up after four. The choke is forgotten (left on 6 s too long) with `mistakeChance(0.4, choke)`.
- **Skills.** A clean bite and a restart that catches count as `starting` practice; the choke pushed off in time as `choke`. `Skills.practice` takes a `scale` and practice here counts three times an ordinary bit (`gainScale`), still limited by the skill's own 30 s gap, so a sitting is worth up to about 0.1 and twelve sittings take Jess from 0.45 to about 0.8 (tested).
- **What is only pretend.** The clutch pedal is not animated and the quad stays in neutral: the bite is a throttle and a timer, and the stall is `engine.stall('practice')`. Nothing is moved by the "clutch". The quad is flagged `practising` so neither girl is made sad by a stall she meant (`companionVoice.watchEngines`).
- **Ends** when the time is up (usually left running, 20% key off), when you come within 3 m (she is slow to get off), when you get on it, when she is asked off, or when it will not restart. The seated girl is held like the warm-up and ready-and-waiting ones (`readyHolding`), so nothing else revs it or switches it off.
- **Panel and console.** Tune group "Practising the clutch", "practising the clutch" under Her state, `practise` under Make her do (the picked vehicle if it has a clutch, else the nearest), `practising` in the console. Lines are in `practisingLines.js` and need recording.
- **Not checked:** how it looks and sounds in the running game. The numbers are first guesses.

## Memories that change choices (#15, was TODO 120)

Agreed with Jamie: she remembers outcomes, a multiplier per kind of offer nudges the chooser, it carries at half strength, she says so now and then, and the Why folder shows and sets it.

- `src/npc/memory.js` (pure, tested): per girl, a multiplier on an offer kind's weight, 1 meaning no feeling. `OUTCOMES` says what each event does to which kinds (bogged: drive -0.25, big go -0.2; tipped: drive and big go -0.3, climb -0.2; won a race or drag +0.25, lost -0.15; a record climb +0.3, a climb +0.1; a clean cone round +0.25, a messy one -0.1; a prank that lands +0.2). It is applied in `updateMinds` beside the resume boost and the mood's keenness, so urgent things (shelter) are nudged too but nothing is ever forced: with its limits at 0.5 and 1.6 a feeling can't make her refuse or insist on anything.
- Bound to the kind of thing, not the place. "Bogged on the hill" is a general wariness of driving; per-place memory is a bigger item and was left.
- Fades at `memoryFade` a second towards 1: Amy 0.0005 (a bad bog is gone in about 15 minutes), Jess 0.0008. The brief said about a game-day, but a day here is a single sitting, so it is minutes. Strength, limits, fade, the line threshold and its chance are `memory*` params in each girl's group, with ranges.
- Saved with the girls (`captureGirls`/`restoreGirls`, `memories` by girl) at `memoryKept` 0.5 of the way from 1 (a farm param), so an overnight bog is half as wary and a save from before this build loads with none. Not a `💥 breaking` change.
- Lines: `memoryWary` and `memoryKeen` for each girl (four each, `mindLines.js`), said when she picks something she feels at least 0.2 either side of 1 about, at `memoryLineChance` 0.3 (Amy with a 120 s cooldown). They are generic across kinds, not one per kind.
- Panel and console: Why shows "remembers" and a closed folder of a slider per kind; the "forgets how everything has gone" action is in Make her do and the console's `girl <name> forget`; `girl.<name>.memory.<kind>` is settable.
- Hooks: her first call for help after a bog or tip (`onStranded`), a race's result, a drag's finish, a hill climb's and cone course's finish, and her prank being found. Not covered: crashes that don't end in a tip, being caught out by something you did, and the jump score.
- Not checked: how it reads in play over a long game; the numbers are first guesses.

## Asking you along (#17, was TODO 122)

Agreed with Jamie: four asks, answered from her wheel, and a no that depends on the girl.

- It gates a choice instead of adding one. `src/npc/askAlong.js` (pure, tests in `tests/askAlong.test.js`): when the chooser picks `rest` (the bales), `flask`, `fire` or `race` (the last only when you are riding near, so the race has you in it), `AskAlong.shouldAsk` may turn the pick into an ask instead of `take()`. Chance is `chance` 1 times her `askChance` (Amy 1.3, Jess 0.8) times her want of company, so a lonely girl asks, a content one just goes. Needs you within `hearRange` 16 m, `startAfter` 90 s, her cooldown 200 s, one ask open at a time, never while they are fallen out. No new offer kind, so nothing in `OFFER_KINDS` changed.
- While it is open she is held (`hold('askAlong')`, so nothing else picks her) and looks at you; if anything else moves her off idle the ask is dropped. Her wheel gets the yes (labelled for the thing) above "Not now". Ignoring it for `lapseAfter` 40 s counts as a not now. The chooser's closure is kept and run on the answer (the race one stripped of you for a girl going alone), after checking she is still free.
- A yes: she goes with you, favour +0.5. A no is her `askGoesAnyway` (Amy 0.2, Jess 0.9), scaled down to `neglectedAnyway` 0.5 of that for a fully neglected girl: she shrugs, says so, goes alone now, nothing lost. Otherwise she sulks: `favour.sulk` for 150 s (up to double when neglected), favour -0.6 (up to double), and she goes alone 25 to 60 s later if she is free then. The sulk is the favour module's mood, not the cab sulk, so it stays small.
- Reasons for the shape: the lines she picks, the walking, the race set-up and all their guards stay in the modules that own them. The asked-for thing does not wait on you getting to it; the yes is the company, since the player has no way to sit on the bales or stand at the fire.
- Debug: "asking you along" under Her state, "asks you along (bales)" under Make her do (`girl <name> ask-along`), `girl.<name>.askingAlong` in the console, the "Asking you along" Tune group and `askChance`, `askGoesAnyway` in each girl's own group. Lines in `askAlongLines.js`, recorded with the rest.
- Not checked: the whole thing in the running game, how often she asks in a long game, and the race ask end to end. The numbers are first guesses.

## Jealousy as a need (#18, was TODO 123)

Decided without asking, as the issue settles the shape: a new need, and neglect made out of it.

- `attention` is a seventh need (`needs.js`, "your attention"). It rises `needJealousRise` 0.006 a second while her friend is within `needCloseRange` 6 m and you are not within `needAttentionRange` 8 m (about three minutes to full), 2.5 times faster (`needJealousFriend`) while you are within 8 m of her friend. It falls `needAttentionFall` 0.05 a second with you near her or her on your quad, fades `needAttentionFade` 0.004 a second with no friend near, and has two jumps: `needSlightBump` +0.2 when you talk to her friend with her stood there (beside the old favour loss), `needTalkEase` -0.3 when you talk to her.
- The neglected switch is replaced. `Favour.standing` takes its negative side from `setWant` (her attention need, set each frame in `updateMinds`), so everything that read neglected (refusing, sulking, the snub, pranks, photos, voice lines, sulkDrive) now builds and fades with it. Favoured still comes from the scores. A bad phone call's mood (`moodDrag`) still pulls her down, and a girl whose friend is locked in by romance stays fully neglected, as before. Scores can still go negative (they still feed her friend's standing), but a low score alone no longer makes her neglected.
- Showing off and pranks likelier: nine offer kinds meet `attention` in `OFFER_KINDS` (showOff 1, prank 0.8, favour 0.8, conker, dare, raceDare, dragDare 0.6, nick and snub 0.5), so a jealous girl scores them higher; the pranks module's `neglectedBoost` stays on top.
- Shown in Why as a slider and in the console as `girl.<name>.needs.attention`; the `needJealous*`, `needAttention*`, `needSlightBump` and `needTalkEase` params are in each girl's Tune group. Not saved, like the other needs.
- Not checked: how long a jealous spell lasts in a real game, or by eye. The numbers are first guesses; the girls' own rates (Jess vs Amy) are the same for now.

## Teaching her something new (#19, was TODO 124)

Agreed with Jamie (all as recommended): two new skills, a "Watch me" slice on her wheel, learning only if she was looking, and the skills changing what she does.

- **Two skills, `bumpStart` and `doubleDeclutch`** (`SHOWN_SKILLS` in `skills.js`), beside the four she learns while driving (`DRIVING_SKILLS`). Jess starts at 0 for both. Amy starts at 0 for bump-starting but 0.95 for double-declutching, a change from the "0 for both" settled in the issue: she already double-declutches, and 0 would have had her grinding every gear from the first minute. `meanSkill` (the L plates' and "Confident" measure) now averages only the four driving skills, so adding two she hasn't been taught doesn't push her confidence down.
- **Asking** (`watchWheelOptions`, game.js): the slice shows when she is stood about (idle, free when asked) within `watchRange` 20 m and there is something to show: "Watch me bump-start it" on a manual with a dead engine you are riding, "Watch me double-declutch" on a box with `crashGears` with the engine running. She is held (`c.hold('watching')`) and given an `attend` on your vehicle (or you) at `watchWeight` 1.2, so a louder notice can still pull her eye away.
- **Looking** (`src/npc/watching.js`, pure, tested): each frame the game says whether her attention pick (`lookPick`) is her focus, you, or your vehicle. When you do the thing, the share of the last `showSeconds` 8 she spent looking has to reach `lookedShare` 0.6. Looking away early is forgiven, looking away just before is not. She gives up after `watchSeconds` 45.
- **Doing it:** a bump-start is the engine firing with `caughtBy === 'roll'`; a double-declutch is `select` returning `ok` into a crash gear above `crashSpeed` (the quad sets `declutched`, which the game reads and clears).
- **What it teaches:** `Skills.watch` adds `watchGain` 0.25 (coaching is 0.12) and pleases her by `taughtFavour`. A miss costs nothing and there is no nagging gap.
- **What the skills change:** `PushStart` takes her `bumpStart`: effort is 0.5 + level (0.5 to 1.5), scaling her shove and how long she lasts, so 0.5 is as she always pushed. `grindsGear` grinds with a chance of `mistake(level) / 2` when the shifter has a `declutchLevel` (set on the manual quads she drives), so 0 always grinds, 1 never and the shifter's old fixed Jess-grinds, Amy-doesn't is the fallback. The grind is still a count and a line, not a real crunch.
- **Panel and console:** "Watching you" tune group; the Girls tab and `girl <name> watch-bump-start` / `watch-double-declutch` start a watch from anywhere; the two skills appear in Skills sliders and `girl.<name>.skills` on their own. Saved with the other skills (a save without them loads at their starting levels).
- **Lines:** `watchOk`, `watchLearnt`, `watchMissed`, `watchGaveUp` for each girl (`watchLines.js`).
- **Not checked:** how it looks and feels in the running game (whether she turns to face you, whether 8 s and 0.6 are right), the wheel by eye, or a bump-start on the tractor.

## Her own day (#20, was TODO 125)

Settled without questions: the issue was clear, and the numbers are first guesses.

- `src/npc/agenda.js` (pure, tested): twelve `AGENDAS`, each a label and the offer kinds (chooser.js) that meet it (the ramp is `jumps`, a race is `race` and `raceDare`, tea is `tea` and `flask`, the Land Rover is `warmCab`). Each girl deals one at the start of a game from `agendaOdds` in characters.js (Jess the loud, quick ones; Amy tea, the bench, the cones, tidying), so personality is odds, not rules.
- **Boost.** `Agenda.boost` gives `agendaBoost` (1.8) on those kinds, multiplied into the offer's weight in `updateMinds` beside the resume boost, the memory and the mood, all game and never fading. Urgent things (shelter) are not on any agenda, so nothing is forced and needs still decide.
- **Mentioning it.** After `agendaMention` (60 s) times half to one and a half, the first time she `canRemark` she says `agenda_<id>` once (one line per agenda for each girl, `mindLines.js`). If she is busy at the time it waits until she is free.
- **Not saved.** It is "today's" mood of mind, so it is rolled on every load, not in `captureGirls`; a farm continued the next day gives her a new one. The farm's character is still deterministic, but the agenda uses `Math.random`, like her starting needs.
- **Panel and console:** Why shows "has in mind today"; `girl <name> agenda` says it now (Make her do); `girl.<name>.agenda` is settable (including `none`).
- **Not checked:** how it reads in play (whether 1.8 is noticeable without being a rut), and the new lines are recorded but not heard.

## Making up (#21, was TODO 126)

No questions: the issue settles it (tea or the better quad, an offer that meets company so the chooser decides when).

- `src/npc/makeUp.js` (pure, `tests/makeUp.test.js`). While they are fallen out, `makeUpTea` and `makeUpQuad` are offers (both meet company 1, purpose 0.4). The girl at fault may offer once the row has lasted `firstAfter` 45 s (`FallingOut.elapsed`, new), the other after a further `otherAfter` 90 s. She must be free and her friend stood or sat about within `range` 60 m. The quad is her own favourite, only if it is parked, upright, unridden and her friend could drive it. Weights `weight` 0.8, quad times `quadWeight` 1. It never starts anything itself; the debug panel's `forced` is the only other way in.
- She walks to talking distance (`sneakTo`, a mug in her hand for tea, the same mug and carry as tea on the rack), says her line, and `acceptChance` 0.85 decides. Taken: her friend answers, takes the mug and has a sip, or with a quad goes and drives it, and `FallingOut.end()` runs with no `madeUp` banter on top (that exchange is an apology, which this replaces). Turned down: her friend says not yet, she says her bit, the row carries on and she does not try again for `refusedCooldown` 150 s. Walk-apart is off for both while one is on her way.
- Lines: `makeUpTea`, `makeUpQuad`, `makeUpTaken`, `makeUpRefused`, `makeUpTurnedDown` for each girl (`fallingOutLines.js`).
- Left alone: it is not in the save (a row not made up still leaves them cool the next morning, as before); the girl sulking in a cab is not free so she does not make a move until she is out; no favour change from being offered tea.
- Checked: unit tests and the smoke test. Not checked in the running game: the walk, the mug changing hands, or how the pace feels; the numbers are guesses.

## Sound shapes what they notice (#22, was TODO 127)

Settled without questions: the issue says what dulls hearing; the numbers are first guesses.

- `hearingFactor` (`attention.js`, pure, tested) gives each girl a 0.25 to 1 share of the range of the sounds she only hears (`heard: true` in `NOTICE_KINDS`). Rain takes up to `hearRain` (0.5) in a full downpour, being inside the barn `hearBarn` (0.35), and a running engine within `hearEngineRange` (12 m) up to `hearEngine` (0.6), fading with distance. They multiply, and `hearFloor` (0.25) is the least she keeps, so a bang still carries a little.
- It scales the range in `candidates`, so a pop 35 m off in the rain goes unheard while one at 5 m does not. Things she has to see (a lurch, a jump, steam) are unchanged. Her own engine counts: a girl on a running quad hears less than one stood by it.
- The tunables join "What catches the eye". The Girls tab shows "hearing" under her state, and `girl.<name>.hearing` reads it in the console.
- Not checked in play: how it reads, or whether the rain is dull enough to notice.


## Getting attached to a quad (#23, TODO 128)

Decided with you: care attaches to the quad she owns (the claim in `ownership.js`), not to whichever she drives most; neglect is a quad in use on almost no fuel or thrashed; care feeds favour as well as lines.

- `src/npc/quadCare.js` (pure, tested; tunables "Caring for her quad"). One care score per girl, 0 to 1, starting at `startCare` 0.4. Someone else at the controls of her running quad within `noticeRange` 45 m: under `lowFuel` 6% of the tank for `dryDelay` 4 s she says `quadDry`; throttle over 0.95 or revs over 92% of the redline for `thrashFor` 6 s (easing off wears the count down at 2 s a second) she says `quadThrashed`. At most one complaint every `speakCooldown` 120 s per girl. Her own driving never counts.
- Each complaint costs her care (0.06 dry, 0.04 thrashed), so a girl let down again and again stops bothering below `minCare` 0.12. Hosing her quad adds 0.01 care a second, and after `proudAfter` 5 s she says `quadProud`, at most every 240 s.
- Favour: washing her quad earns `favourWashPerSecond` 0.1 a second scaled by her care, on top of the existing favourite-quad wash score. If it is you thrashing it and she says so, you lose `favourThrash` 1.2 scaled by her care. Falling out is untouched: Jess or Amy thrashing it is words only.
- Kept overnight (`captureGirls` `care`), in full; an old save without it starts at `startCare`. Girls tab "Her state" shows it, and the console has `girl.<name>.quadCare`.
- Lines are `{q}` templates (colour or nickname), so each is written out per quad. Not checked in the running game: how often she speaks up, or whether 6 s flat out is right.

## The girls at the pub (#358)

The banter's two new situations, both raised by `PubVisit` (`src/npc/pubVisit.js`, pure, tested) and added to the triggers in `updateBanter`. `atPub` is a lasting situation: you stopped (a quad under 2 m/s, or on foot) within 14 m of `village.pub.stop` with both girls within 14 m and settled (stood, resting or sat as passengers, not driving). It starts an exchange now and then (gap 100 s, 5% a second), after the idle one in priority so the pub's chat wins over the general standing about. `leavingPub` is a moment: after 8 s there together, you pull away (over 2 m/s) or get 20 m off, with both girls within 20 m; it speaks once and the visit resets. Leaving them behind, or riding past without stopping, says nothing.
- **Lines.** Eight waiting chats and five goodbyes in `pubLines.js`, merged at the end of the banter so earlier recordings are untouched. They mention drinks the girls would have (Amy a lemonade, Jess cider) and that the door is painted on, but nothing is drunk: no new mechanic, no need or favour change.
- **Not done.** The girls do not go into the pub or get a drink in hand; that waits until the door opens.
- **Debug.** "Girls at the pub" in the Tune tab (the four distances and times); the village group in the Girls tab and `village stayed` in the console show the seconds at the pub.

## A start-of-day briefing (#45, was TODO 150)

- Decided: Amy reads out the day soon after the game loads, and Jess adds her plan, rather than each girl mentioning things at random later. Turning hidden state into story was the point.
- `src/npc/briefing.js` (pure, tested): `planBriefing` makes the parts (an opener, the weather, the board's promise, the worst quad or "nothing wrong", Amy's agenda, Jess's agenda); `Briefing` hands them out one at a time when nobody is talking and the speaker `canRemark`, after `briefingGap` 0.8 s, dropping a part nobody can say for `briefingGiveUp` 40 s. It starts `briefingAfter` 8 s in.
- It reuses the existing weather, forecast and agenda lines (the weather choice moved to `weatherSituation` and companionVoice uses it too), so only three new situations were recorded: `briefingOpen`, `briefingAllWell` and a quad line `briefingTrouble` (per quad and nickname, so Amy calls it what the farm does).
- "Playing up" is the highest started fault level over all the girls' quads, at least `briefingTroubleFrom` 0.25. Quirks are not counted: they are the quad's character, not today's trouble.
- Once it has run, the girls' own weather and forecast remarks are marked said, and the agenda's own timer is skipped for the girls it covered. It runs each game, not each day, and is not saved.
- Debug: "Briefing" in the Tune tab, a readout and button in the Girls tab, `briefing` in the console.

## A shirt and tie (#489)

- **Dealt** about one day in ten for each girl (`shirtTie` in `outfitOdds`), white or pale blue, over the polo half the time (`shirtOverPoloChance`, Outfits tuning), loosened with her own odds (`tieLooseChance`: Amy 0.2, Jess 0.8), one or two buttons undone and the knot 4 to 6 cm down. It takes the place of whatever else she'd have worn, from a stream of its own (`(farmSeed ^ 0x7e1e) + day * 7919`), so every other day's outfit is as before. Dealing its numbers from the outfit stream moved Jess's whole deal on the smoke test's farm (a polo became a plaid over it) and took the wood 21,000 triangles over budget; from its own stream it's 4.03M. On its own it costs 10,800 triangles, over the polo 18,500, as a buttoned plaid does.
- **The shirt** (`SHIRT` in `polo.js`): the polo's builder, tucked in, cuffs at the wrist with a button on the outside of each (`cuffButtons`), seven white buttons to the waistband, and a chest pocket on her left laid on the shirt by rays (`buildPocket`). Loosened (`SHIRT_ROLLED`) the sleeves are rolled to the elbow. The buttons down the middle aren't drawn (`underTie`): under the tie they showed through it as she moved.
- **The tie** (`src/npc/tie.js`, pure, tested) is laid on the finished shirt by rays from in front, each row flat across the highest of what's under it, falling back off her bust no faster than 0.55 m per metre and coming back to rest against her over its last 7 cm, to its point 4 mm above the waistband. The blade widens from 1.3 cm under the knot to 8 cm. The knot is 3 cm high, 3.4 cm across its top, standing 1.4 cm out, shaded with the crease of its last turn and a dimple under it. Done right up its top is 1.7 cm above the shirt's neck, up in the collar's stand. Loosened, its two ends run up from the knot into the collar.
- **The collar** (`POINT_COLLAR`): a taller, stiffer stand and points falling down more than out. Its wings start 5 mm from her middle (`fallGap`), so they lie over the knot's sides. Over the polo, the polo is done up and its collar lies close under the shirt's (`POLO_UNDER_SHIRT_COLLAR`), so its placket and collar show in a loosened V.
- **What she says**: the first time you open her wheel on a day she's in it (`outfitRemark`), Amy that she's going into town later and Jess whether you like it.
- Panel: "tie loosened" and "over the polo" in each girl's clothes; console `tieLoose` and `shirtOverPolo`; `?outfit=shirtTie`.

### Follow-up: less loose, a smaller collar, the buttons, and her elbows (testing #494)

Jamie, after a first look: the loosened tie was too loose, the collar looked odd, none of the buttons showed, and sitting on a bale her skin came through the shirt. The tie stays her polo's colour.

- **Loosened** now has only the top button undone and the knot 2 to 3 cm down (was one or two, and 4 to 6), sitting over the bottom of the open neck rather than below it.
- **The collar** is smaller (`POINT_COLLAR`: stand 2.4 cm, was 3; points about 4 cm long, were 5). Its points lie on the shirt: from a third of the way down the fall each point is pressed on to what's under it (`lie`), where before it was only ever lifted off it and so stood out in the air. The dark edge is gone (`edgeShade` 1): on a white shirt it read as a line drawn round the collar.
- **The buttons** were the plaid's all along (the same placket builder), but the ones under the tie weren't drawn because they showed through it. They're all drawn now, and the tie stands 5 mm off the placket (was 4.5), clear of a button's 2.6 mm.
- **Her elbows.** Sat on a bale, the long sleeve was squeezed on the inside of her bent elbow and her forearm came through. The sleeve has more room now (1.6 cm, was 1.1; at the cuff 1.3, was 0.8), with 7 mm more round the elbow (`elbowEase`), and more again over the polo. Over the polo none showed in the shots afterwards; on its own a hairline still did on the inside of the bend.
